  **THE WIKI'S MOST-CITED ADOPTION SERIES GOES FROM SECOND-HAND TO PRIMARY — AND TWO OF THE THREE CITATIONS IT ALREADY CARRIED DO NOT SURVIVE THE CHECK.** The Ramp AI Index appeared in four places (`SPCX`, `themes/tokenmaxxing`, `themes/macro-cycle`, and reconciliation entry #8 of 08-17-night) always via someone else's note. With the file on disk: median AI spend per employee **$2.32 ➔ $11.95 (5.15x)**, top decile **$62.02 ➔ $650.00 (10.48x)**, top 1% **$858.59 ➔ $7,400.50 (8.62x)**, and the growth rate itself stepping up across three 12-month regimes (median compound **+2.25 ➔ +3.78 ➔ +8.79 %/mo**), with **63% of the median cohort's three-year dollar gain in the last six months**. ✓ **Goldman (08-14) pinned:** its *"$5 ➔ $12 / $240 ➔ $650"* pair is based on the **Dec-25** print, so the move is **+127% / +169%**, not the +140% / +171% the rounding implies — "more than doubled" survives, the magnitude is restated on `macro-cycle`. ⚠️ **MS (via `SPCX`) y/y does not reproduce:** levels tie, rates do not (+154.8% vs +167%; +215.4%/+200.9% vs "~240-250% each"). ⚠️⚠️ **The "06-18 cut" (median $11 / top-10% $611) is not on this series at all** — $611 appears in no month; on 06-18 the newest print was May-26 ($10.50 / $465.39). **Reconciliation #8 of 08-17-night is partly superseded**: the GS chain checks out against primary, the 06-18 mark does not. - **2026-08-11** · `/wiki-ingest` (scheduled 21h; `_default` + 32 touched tickers → **08-11**, plus 13 screened-no-change tickers advanced off the old 08-10 default) · **sources: Outlook 438 inbox emails in the 08-10 20:00 → 08-11 window, keyword-triaged to 38 whitelist-TMT items, all 38 full bodies fetched · Twitter/X corpus 0 NEW POSTS — the corpus is DEAD, not quiet (see the flag below) · 0 new transcripts under `E:\ASML - Copia\transcripts` · 0 new files in `_equity_calls`; `_inbox` NOT touched (3 files dated 08-11 belong to the 23h `/run-inbox`)** · **32 company pages + 5 theme pages touched** (LITE CRWV SMCI NVDA AMAT LRCX KLAC ASML TSM SAMSUNG SKHYNIX INTC AAPL MEDIATEK ANTHROPIC GLW DELL MRVL AVGO AMD COHR MSFT GOOG META OPENAI SPCX CRM APP PLTR ORCL KIOXIA APH; themes semicap-wfe / custom-asic-tpu / hbm-memory / optical-cpo / ai-datacenter-power) · **1 new `## Changelog` section created ([[CRM]] — the page had none)** · headline events:

  **① THREE PRINTS, AND ALL THREE SAY THE SAME THING ABOUT THE BAR.** [[LITE]] beat revenue, GM, OM and EPS ($1.006–1.010B / 50.4% / 36.6% / $3.23 vs $988–990M / 48.5–48.8% / 35.2–35.5% / $2.97) **and** guided September revenue ~8% and EPS ~16% above Street ($1.225–1.275B, mid $1.250B; EPS mid $4.20), reaching its target model *"more than a quarter ahead of schedule"* — and traded **flat-to-down**. JPM's diagnosis is the transferable one: *"the tepid response feels like an **expectations problem**… by the end of the day today folks were saying the company had to do **$1.3b** in revenue guide to work… **reminiscent of the TXN print**."* A buy-side expert call (Papa Sylla) had published exactly that $1.3B bar **hours before the print**. [[SMCI]] guided **FY27 to $65–72B against a $54.4B Street (~+26%)** with **GM 17.6%** — above FY25's 11.2% *and* FY24's 13.9%, breaking the "commodity assembly margin" bear — and got **+7%**, because the **>$60bn of bookings pre-announced on 07-21 had already paid for it**. [[CRWV]] delivered the operating-leverage inflection both bulls and bears had underwritten against (**adj. OM ~5% vs a ~3% bogey**, EBITDA 59%, **active power +~500MW to 1.5GW vs a +250–350MW bogey**) and got **+10%**, but reported backlog grew only ~$4.6B to $104B with **$9.4B of capex against $679M of CFO**. ➜ **THE OPERATIONAL CONCLUSION: in this tape, clearing published research is not sufficient — the marginal buyer trades off an intraday whisper. Filed on all three pages and on `themes/optical-cpo.md` as the set-up warning into [[COHR]]'s 08-12 print.**

  **② THE $500BN PLATFORM'S MECHANICS ARRIVED, AND THEY ARE BETTER THAN THE HEADLINE — WHILE THE CREDIT MARKET WENT THE OTHER WAY.** [[NVDA]] provides *"a **'residual support mechanism' for up to 25%** of the investments… **limited, residual value based**, and designed to complement — not replace — independent underwriting"*; partners underwrite each deal on its merits with **no decisions made yet**; and the base rate is small — **the 10-Q puts total partner lease-guarantee exposure at $3.5bn** (MS / Joseph Moore, OW PT $288). BofA (Buy, Top Pick, PO $320): *"**NVDA underwrites the value, not the loan**… a **$500bn pool (2.5x Google's network)** lets non-investment-grade buyers — labs, neoclouds, sovereigns — secure GPUs, power and data centers at attractive rates,"* sizing the *prior* vendor-financing programme at only **~15% of ~$470bn of CY26-27 FCF**. **NET-NEW UPSIDE NOBODY MODELS: a 35% revenue share could be ">$10bn per GW/yr of 100% margin revenue," worth 10%+ to FY29 EPS.** ⚠️ **AND THE DISSENT, which is now a genuine asset-class disagreement: NVDA's CDS widened +5bps to 77bps vs CDX IG at 52bps, WSJ reports ~0.40pts over Treasuries demanded on NVDA paper, and BofA CREDIT cut [[AVGO]] to Marketweight from OW on "more complicated spread and RESIDUAL-VALUE risk" from XPV financing — the same structure that produced a +7% equity re-rating in Broadcom. Stratechery's "Nvidia's Risky Business" states the bear precisely: backstopping residual values "drawing on INSURANCE FLOATS AND PENSION CAPITAL… echoing the RAILROAD BOND MANIA OF THE 1870s that ended in the Panic of 1873."** **THE FORWARD TEST FILED ON THE PAGE: classify each announced platform deal as a GPU-USAGE backstop or a CREDIT guarantee — Moore says "judging 25%, and passivity, will be assessed one deal at a time" — and track the 10-Q lease-guarantee line ($3.5bn today) as the scoreboard.** ⚠️ **Sizing conflict logged and NOT resolved: Larry Fink puts each GW at $50-60bn and US need at >70GW, which implies $500bn ≈ 8-10 GW all-in — 28–54% HIGHER cost/GW than the $39bn/GW anchor this wiki used on 08-10 to get 10-13 GW. The discrepancy makes the $500bn look SMALLER relative to need, not larger.**

  **③ THE SEMICAP REVISION GOT ITS TARGETS — AND HIGH-NA QUIETLY BECAME A ONE-CUSTOMER MARKET.** Bernstein's WFE raise ($148bn / $204bn / $259bn for 2026/27/28, from $141bn / $175bn / $198bn) was already on `themes/semicap-wfe.md` from 08-10; the 08-11 desk distribution added the marks: **[[AMAT]] PT $525 → $675 (+29%)**, a ranking of **AMAT > [[LRCX]] > [[KLAC]]** (all OP; LRCX/KLAC PTs raised but **NOT quantified in the relays — deliberately not inferred**), **[[ASML]] the EU top pick on a 34% top-line CAGR 25–28E**, and — a first — **[[SPCX]]'s Terafab named as a quantified driver inside a third-party WFE model** (AMAT's page had carried it as *"not yet in numbers"*). Composition: **DRAM +53%/+37%, NAND +58%/+42%, logic-foundry +20%/+19%**. Separately **JPM/Harlan Sur raised the semi industry forecast to $1.68T CY26 / $2.25T CY27 on a THIRD straight month of accelerating WSTS sales "led by microcomponents, analog, and sensors & actuators"** — the non-AI leg turning, which matters for durability. **[[INTC]]'s raise was upsized $15bn → ~$20bn at $95 (−6.5%) against $100bn+ of demand, its first since 1971**, and the complex re-rated only on 08-11 (**ASML +4% / KLAC +4% / LRCX +3% / AMAT +1%**) after being **down 2-3% on 08-10** — i.e. the market waited for size and demand before calling it credible capex. ⚠️🔴 **THE STRUCTURAL ITEM: [[SAMSUNG]] pushed High-NA EUV out to ~1nm (≈2030) from 2nm/1.4nm on "technical hurdles and cost pressures"; [[TSM]] has bought High-NA for R&D only and says 2029 A12/A13 won't need it; TrendForce — "only one outlier in early High-NA EUV adoption." The High-NA book is INTC alone into ~2029–30, so the $20bn raise and the High-NA order book are now the same trade. Bernstein's logic-foundry growth is a LOW-NA + DUV-immersion number and must not be read as High-NA.**

  **④ THE ASIC SECOND-SOURCING WENT FROM A THESIS TO A PATTERN AT *THREE* LAYERS.** [[MEDIATEK]]'s 2027 AI-ASIC share guide (10-15% → 15-20%, already logged 07-31) now has a dollar value — **~$14bn of 2027 revenue at the midpoint** (DB / Robert Sanders, Buy TP TWD5,000) — plus **448G SerDes IP ready H2-27 for an expected TPU v10 in 2029**, and MS calling the guide *"broadly in line with Joe Moore's assumption of a 20% revenue share in TPU programs."* **GUC** enters at the design-service layer, and **Hon Hai is taking TPU assembly share from Celestica** (CPU tray L10 for V7 this year → L6/L10/L11 for V8 in 2027 → *"bigger with TPU V9 in 2028E"*) explicitly because *"CLS's capacity expansion plan is too slow."* ➜ **[[GOOG]] is dual-sourcing silicon, design services AND assembly — bullish for TPU volume DELIVERABILITY (the mechanism that makes the aggressive 2028 targets physical) and a share risk for every incumbent.** ⚠️ **A model has [[MRVL]] losing its ENTIRE Trainium3 allocation in 2027 (Fubon: 2mn Trn3 units in both years, AWS/Alchip/MRVL 0.4/1.2/0.4 → 0.4/1.6/ZERO), directionally corroborated by Alchip's July revenue +108% m/m to NT$7.43bn (from +85% in June). Single-broker forecast; the tests are MRVL's 08-27 print and Alchip's monthly slope.** **And [[MSFT]]'s Maia got sized and found wanting: 300k+ units sought for 2027 (ambition 1mn+) against [[GOOG]]'s 12-15M TPU target by 2028 and [[AMZN]]'s ~1 million Trainium2 already running Claude via Project Rainier — "a distant third." [[MRVL]] popped +4% on it and faded.**

  **⑤ MEMORY: A SUPPLIER SAYS ONLY 60% OF BIG-TECH DEMAND IS BEING MET AND LTA TENORS ARE GOING 3y → 5y, WHILE THE FLOWS DATA SHOWS INVESTORS STILL SELLING IT.** Jefferies/Jeff Kim: *"As of Aug 2026, **only 60% of big techs' memory demand is being met. The shortage is worsening**… should persist for **at least three years** [fabs take 3+ years]. Accordingly, **hyperscalers are pursuing 5y LTAs instead of 3y LTAs**, while also requesting a 5y base term with 1y rollover"* — with [[SAMSUNG]] 3Q26 OP forecast at **KRW112tn (+817% y/y, 55% OPM)**, **DRAM OPM to 83%**, **NAND OPM to 71%**, and **foundry breakeven for the first time since 2022** on 4nm LPU; he also expects SEC to announce a **shareholder return of KRW100-200tn vs KRW9.8tn today (>10x, dividend yield >7%)**, on which the stock rose 4%. ⚠️ **Against that, MS's prime-brokerage data shows memory still being SOLD / DE-GROSSED "as investors contemplate PEAK RATE OF CHANGE and whether LTAs are 'good or bad'," with hyperscalers taking the flows. Two desks, same week, opposite conclusions — logged side by side; the empirical test is whether any buyer or producer confirms the 3y→5y tenor.** 🔴 **NET-NEW AND POSSIBLY UNDER-WEIGHTED: "memory chips account for roughly 62% of the cost of Nvidia's Vera Rubin… SOCAMM2, NOT HBM4, leads the bill" (DIGITIMES) — which means the HBM stack-height de-spec debate is only part of the cost question, and cross-checks awkwardly against the SOCAMM 1.5TB→768GB revision logged 08-09.**

  **① THE QUOTE OF THE SEASON, AND IT IS A SUPPLY-CHAIN RISK STATED BY THE BUYER.** [[MSFT]] CFO Amy Hood, asked by Bernstein how Microsoft protects itself against overbuild: capex has "really pivot[ed] toward… **SHORT-LIVED ASSETS, which really, that's CPUs and GPUs**… **IF THE DEMAND ENVIRONMENT CHANGES, YOU JUST SLOW DOWN WHAT IS, IN FACT, THE LARGEST COMPONENT AND THE DRIVER OF COGS**," and "**when you have the ability to LATE BIND some of the more expensive components in short term, you have a big book of business that's FLEXIBLE**" (via Culpium/Tim Culpan, 08-02). ⚠️ **[[AMZN]]'s Jassy said the equivalent in his own words ("we typically purchase these A FEW MONTHS BEFORE putting them into service… IF THE DEMAND ISN'T THERE, WE WON'T SPEND THE CAPITAL") and [[META]]'s Susan Li left 2028 "wide open," framed as securing land and power while DEFERRING CHIPS. Three of the four largest buyers described the same option inside one fortnight. Culpan's read — "a game of capacity chicken… executives are incentivized to PUBLICLY MAINTAIN THEIR LONG-TERM CAPEX TARGETS WHILE FIDDLING WITH THE ACTUAL AMOUNT OF COMPUTE THEY INSTALL quarter-to-quarter" — makes headline capex a deliberately uninformative number. THE OPERATIONAL CONCLUSION FILED ON themes/hyperscaler-capex: track capex MIX (short- vs long-lived, which Microsoft now discloses at ~two-thirds short-lived) and the shell-build-vs-GPU-install divergence, not the headline. Counterweight logged with equal weight: all four RAISED 2026 capex, MSFT guided F1Q27 capex >$50bn from $41bn, and MS's supply-chain team reads the raises as hyperscalers "raising spending to ABSORB [component] costs RATHER THAN CUTTING UNIT VOLUMES." Late-binding is an option they bought and described, not a decision they made.**

  **② [[GOOG]] IS SYSTEMATICALLY DISMANTLING THE MERCHANT-ASIC BUSINESS MODEL, and JPM says so in words that name [[AVGO]] without naming it: "through the evolution from TPUv8 to v10 families, the BLACK-BOX MODEL FROM US FABLESS VENDOR (WHERE IP, DESIGN SERVICE, MANUFACTURING AND PACKAGING VALUE ADD ARE ALL BUNDLED TOGETHER AT HIGH MARGINS) WILL BE BROKEN DOWN SYSTEMATICALLY IN FAVOR OF A DISAGGREGATED APPROACH"** (JPM · Gokul Hariharan, GUC upgrade to OW, 08-02). Three engagement models are live under the v10 "Icefish" RFQ: semi-CoT with [[MEDIATEK]] on SerDes/D2D/packaging/integration; compute-die back-end to GUC/Alchip on Turnkey-3; and ⚠️ **the full break-up where "ONLY IP BEING SOURCED BY GOOGLE SEPARATELY… WITH FOUNDRY PROCUREMENT BEING HANDLED DIRECTLY BY GOOGLE, SIMILAR TO THE AMAZON TRAINIUM APPROACH" — which would collapse the ASIC vendor's revenue basis toward IP+services and hand the manufacturing margin to Google, who already buys its own HBM. JPM's hierarchy of winners (MediaTek first, GUC/Alchip "somewhat") is the reconciliation with its own MediaTek note published four hours earlier arguing MediaTek does NOT lose content at v10. Award expected by end-2026; MP late 2029.** **AND THE ARM CPU LEG IS THE UNDER-MODELLED HALF: Google DOUBLED PHYSICAL CPU HOSTS PER SERVER — CPU:TPU ratio to 1:2 from 1:4 from TPU v8 on — implying ~4.8MN AXION 2 UNITS across 2026-27 off 9.6mn v8-series TPUs, with next-gen at ~2x ASP and Axion 3 a two-die package. GUC's Google-CPU revenue to ~US$4.6BN BY 2028 (~70% of GUC). ⚠️ JPM names "TSMC's CAPACITY SUFFICIENCY" as the binding risk and "an increase in 3NM ALLOCATION AT TSMC FOR GOOGLE CPUs IN 2027" as a catalyst — N3 is allocation-constrained by a workload the AI narrative barely models. Read-through to [[ARM]] royalties, [[TSM]] N3, [[SAMSUNG]] Foundry (2028-29 share risk) and AI-server BOM (CPU/DRAM/substrate content).**

  **③ [[MEDIATEK]] GETS ITS UNIT-AND-ASP BUILD, AND THE SOTP SHOWS THE CORE BUSINESS IS WORTH ALMOST NOTHING. JPM raised FY27/28 EPS +8%/+7% (NT$137.95/NT$296.89) holding OW/PT NT$5,300, on DC ASIC revenue of US$2.25/15.1/43.3BN for 26/27/28 — 0.5/3.3/1.3mn v8 "Zebrafish" units at ASP $4,250/4,500/4,000 plus 3.0MN v9 "HUMUFISH" UNITS AT ASP US$12,667 (US$38.0bn). ⚠️ The v9 ASP is modelled at ~2.8x v8 and is ~88% of the 2028 ASIC line — the number to attack. ASIC to 11%/45%/70% of revenue, OPM to 16%/22%/26%. Against that, SMARTPHONE UNITS CUT −13%/−21%/−22% to 299/240/230mn, and the SoTP puts the core at NT$416/share (8x) vs NT$4,879 for ASIC (30x) — the handset franchise is ascribed essentially no support. Nomura is Street-high at NT$5,800 (25x blended 27-28 EPS vs a stock at 15x) with estimates UNCHANGED because his US$14bn already sat inside the guide, and adds that the implied 4Q26 GPM of ≥45-46% runs ABOVE his own 44.7% model. EMIB-T yield quantified for the first time: "~60% at SUBSTRATE level and ~90% at CHIP-ON-SUBSTRATE." Second ASIC customer "we believe SPACEX," confirmation before end-2026.**

  **④ EMIB-T'S SUBSTRATE ECONOMICS ONLY WORK BECAUSE [[INTC]] IS PAYING FOR THEM — from Unimicron's own Q2'26 call: substrate MASS PRODUCTION 2027, three vendors (Unimicron/Ibiden/Shinko), INITIAL YIELD TARGET ~50%, and "INTEL WILL PROVIDE COMMERCIAL PROTECTION DURING THE EARLY RAMP, ALLOWING IT TO MAINTAIN REASONABLE PROFITABILITY IN THE LOW YIELDS"** (@IntelProMUltra relay, 08-01). ⚠️ **Two independent sources now bracket EMIB-T substrate yield at 50-60%, and only one mentions the subsidy. JPM treats the yield question as the cleared obstacle; Unimicron's disclosure suggests it is cleared because Intel is absorbing the learning curve — a cost that lands in Foundry GM in exactly the 2027 window the turnaround needs. Timing still unresolved (substrate MP 2027 vs MediaTek's customer HVM 2028); carried both ways.**

  **⑤ [[SAMSUNG]] DISCLOSES THE LTA ASYMMETRY AND IT IS A WRITTEN FLOOR UNDER THE MEMORY CYCLE: "60-70% OF ITS MEMORY SALES WILL BE UNDER LTA but WITH FAVORABLE TERMS (PRICE CUT LIMIT vs BROADLY OPEN FOR PRICE INCREASE; e.g. SUB-5% DOWN QoQ vs 10-20%+ INCREASE)," mostly 5-YEAR ROLLING and "MAINLY FOR US BIG TECH," so "the 6th year can be added right after/before 1st year completion"** (BofA · Simon Woo, 08-02). ⚠️ **Capped downside / uncapped upside on two-thirds of the largest DRAM producer's book means the next downturn hits VOLUMES before PRICES — which invalidates the historical memory-cycle analogues this wiki has been using, and makes the un-disclosed [[SKHYNIX]]/[[MU]] LTA terms the highest-value disclosure gap in the complex (flagged on all three pages). Also: July DRAM contract +LOW-TEENS% MoM / +30-50% QoQ but NAND FLAT — the ASP-extrapolation trade under [[SNDK]]/[[KIOXIA]] is not being validated by the print, and Jefferies' own analyst now says his "bullish NAND vs DRAM" stance "MAY BE SHIFTING." [[LRCX]] NAND revenue nonetheless MORE THAN DOUBLED Q/Q — capacity committed ahead of pricing.**

  **⑥ THE PREVIEW WAVE, AND THE TWO WIDEST BULL-vs-BULL DISAGREEMENTS ON THE WIKI. [[ALAB]] (08-04): Jefferies expects a September guide of "$500M+ vs. Consensus $412M" — ~14-17% ABOVE UBS's own BULL case of $440M from 24 hours earlier — on 500K Trn3 units in Sep/Q and 2.3M lifetime C26/27, which is BELOW UBS's 4.6mn, so the two houses differ on $/XPU in the opposite direction from their ratings; plus a new number, "optical engines for an NPO-enabled Scorpio-X may drive $/XPU WITH Trn4 TO $2K+," against UBS's architectural bear case that AWS migrates off NeuronLink toward NVLink/UALink with [[MRVL]] the beneficiary. [[AMD]] (08-04): Jefferies at June $12.2B+/Sept $13.5B+ vs UBS $11.33B/$12.71B — ~$0.8-0.9bn apart on both — on server volume +~20% q/q "after capacity constrained [[INTC]] printed 9% in 2Q26 with pricing up 10%+," Venice ASPs $3-5K vs ~$1.5K, ⚠️ but "LESS BENEFIT FROM THE MI3xx SERIES AS CUSTOMERS PREPARE FOR HELIOS" (modest 3Q, ramp 4Q26/1Q27), and Helios customers now include [[MSFT]] and [[ANTHROPIC]] alongside [[META]]/[[OPENAI]]. [[ON]] (08-03): Jefferies calls an outright BEAT & RAISE (HSD guide vs cons 5%, 14x C28 cons EPS $5.70, SiC ~55% of new China EV models, DC revenue $250M C25 doubling in C26, GaN opportunity ">$1.5B") and DOES NOT ADDRESS UBS's "losing pricing share while building unit inventories" — logged as the unresolved bear fact. [[SNDK]] (08-05): buyside Sep/Q bar $50-60 EPS on ASP +25-30%, ⚠️ but "signs this stance MAY BE SOFTENING," and the analyst previewing a beat also describes the air pocket — Edge was 62% of March revenue and grew ~120% q/q on a pre-buy, "at a certain point… THAT REVENUE WILL BEGIN TO FALL OFF." [[PLTR]] (08-03): two more desks land on US Commercial as the deciding metric and add "EARLY SIGNS / CHECKS THAT THERE COULD BE SOME COMPETITIVE ENCROACHMENT," while "we haven't heard 'valuation' as a nit from the bear community in quite sometime" at 30x '28 FCF. [[SPCX]] (08-04, first-ever print): Street $6.819BN revenue, ~$10.9BN CASH BURN on $14BN capex, capex to double to ~$25bn by Jun-Q 2027, stock $108.37 (−49% from peak, −20% below the $135 IPO) — with the LOCKUP EXPIRING 08-06 and a contrarian call that shorts are already positioned and will cover.**

  **⑦ THE FIRST DOWNWARD ADVANCED-PACKAGING REVISION OF THE CYCLE, AND IT IS SoIC NOT CoWoS: Fubon cut TSMC's SoIC capacity to 70K WPM BY Q4-28 FROM 90K (35k today) while HOLDING CoWoS at 120k → 220k wpm with upside.** ⚠️ **The split says the marginal 2027-28 AI die is still interposer-attached, not logic-on-logic stacked — direct negative for [[BESI]] hybrid bonders, positive for SUSS on CoWoS/CoPoS, and it pushes the CPO packaging step-up beyond the forecast window (the same desk: "we remain MORE CONSERVATIVE in our expectation for rate of CPO adoption"). Same note: [[NVDA]] RUBIN 2026 VOLUMES CUT TO 1MN UNITS on "THERMAL DESIGN CHANGE AND PRODUCTION PUSH-OUT," with higher Blackwell compensating — the second independent source describing Nvidia's rack-scale roadmap slipping for PHYSICAL-INTEGRATION rather than silicon reasons (cf. SemiAnalysis on Kyber/NVL576). Volume-neutral for TSMC, ASP-negative for Nvidia's 2026 mix.**

  **⑧ THE CAPEX AGGREGATE RE-MARKED TO ~$860BN/$1.2TN WITH $2.3TN OF COMMITMENTS BEHIND IT (BofA · Vivek Arya), AND THE MACRO MEASUREMENT PROBLEM SOLVED THREE WAYS AT ~$1TN GLOBALLY (GS Global Economics · Briggs/Dong).** GS's adjusted build — stripping pre-AI trend, adding private/foreign, allocating geographically — gives **~$1,019bn global / ~$582bn US for 2026**, cross-checked at ~$1,060bn (gross-profit revisions) and ~$1,002bn (national accounts/trade), rising from **1.8% of US GDP in 2026 to 2.5% in 2027 and 2.8% in 2028 — "CONSISTENT WITH THOSE IN PRIOR GENERAL-PURPOSE TECHNOLOGY BUILDOUTS,"** which retires "the sheer size proves a bubble" as an argument. ⚠️ **Two warnings new to the wiki: a trade-data nowcast shows "A MODERATE SLOWDOWN IN INVESTMENT… IN JUNE AND JULY" (the first hard-data deceleration signal here, covering exactly the unwind months), and "recent increases in AI investment have INCREASINGLY BEEN DRIVEN BY COST INFLATION," so nominal capex growth is overstating COMPUTE growth — anyone converting capex dollars to GW or accelerator units now needs a deflator. Duration caveat nobody on the sell side applied: the $2.3tn backlog has 12-month recognition of 30% ([[MSFT]]) and 12% ([[ORCL]]), and the OpenAI-AWS $138bn runs 8 YEARS.**

  **⑨ SEMICAP SERVICE REVENUE IS OUTGROWING EQUIPMENT BY 13PP — "combined service revenue across [[ASML]], Lam, TEL and [[KLAC]] grew 33% Y/Y IN THE JUNE QUARTER AGAINST 20% FOR EQUIPMENT," with LT targets from Lam 10%+ / ASM 12%+ up to KLA 13-15% and [[AMAT]] mid-teens, "the two most recent updates BOTH REVISED UPWARD"** (@SKundojjala). ⚠️ **Service attaches to installed-base UTILISATION, so this is not what a cycle top looks like — set directly against the same weekend's explicit "ASMI cyclical de-rating expected to continue… WEAKNESS OVER THE NEXT 12-15 MONTHS" call and its mechanism, which requires no demand decline at all: "EVEN IF HYPERSCALER CAPEX GROWTH STOPS EXCEEDING EXPECTATIONS AND COMES IN AT PREDICTED STRONG GROWTH RATES, IT COULD SEND THE SEMICONDUCTOR INDUSTRY INTO OVERSUPPLY AND A DOWNCYCLE WITHIN A FEW QUARTERS." Both filed on themes/semicap-wfe with the falsifiable discriminators (DDR5 2027 pricing, inventory build, service growth decelerating toward the equipment rate).**

  **⑩ THE JULY UNWIND IS QUANTIFIED AND RE-DATED, AND THE SALP +80% GETS DECOMPOSED. Gaetano's anatomy: tracked optical names −30-70% in six weeks while SOXX stayed +67.7% YTD; ~$11BN OF US SEMI-FUND OUTFLOWS IN THE WEEK ENDED JUNE 24 — "THE LARGEST WEEKLY WITHDRAWAL THIS CENTURY"; Asia L/S funds −18.6% through 28 July; >1.2MN KOREAN LEVERAGED ACCOUNTS MARGIN-CALLED BY 13 JULY with ~320-360K FULLY LIQUIDATED (~1 in 30 adults), then KOSPI +17.91% ON 31 JULY, its largest one-day gain on record. ⚠️ "So much of this began BEFORE Kimi, before the accelerated Kospi crash, and before the Leo / SA Fund drama" — which DEMOTES the SALP liquidation from cause to accelerant, a correction to how themes/macro-cycle framed it on 07-30/31 and 08-01. And the return decomposition: the public book was "EFFECTIVELY WIPED OUT by leverage… while the fund's REPORTED POSITIVE YTD RETURN WAS LARGELY SUPPORTED BY THE UNREALIZED APPRECIATION OF ITS PRIVATE [[ANTHROPIC]] INVESTMENT" (~75% of NAV to zero + 25% at +620% = +80%). Back-solved, NOT ADOPTED — but it poses the right question for every allocator: how much of 2026 AI-fund performance is one unrealised private mark? Execution detail dates the capitulation: Citadel took a block at a 10% DISCOUNT overnight 07-29 vs a normal 1.5-2.5%. Counterweight logged: @biancoresearch says the fund "STILL EXISTS… STILL UP 80% YTD" and "will raise big dollars," in which case the Anthropic stake was never a forced-sale candidate. Three independent "low is in" calls (22V positioning + JPM TPM at 20th percentile/−1.5z + UBS's mid-August momentum seasonality) against Visser's more durable warning: "MARKET STRUCTURE HAS CHANGED FOREVER… PRIME BROKERS WON'T ALLOW LEVERAGE BACK TO PRIOR LEVELS. EXPECT MORE SPEED CRASHES, NOT MEAN REVERSION."**

  **⑪ Smaller but filed: [[ASML]] — press reports that CXMT, [[SMIC]] and Hua Hong ordered 25 DOMESTIC IMMERSION LITHO SYSTEMS, small in units but the first time the three largest domestic buyers ordered simultaneously, and CXMT's IPO printed +500% DAY ONE so the China memory-capex funding constraint just loosened (⚠️ press-sourced, no vendor named, NOT ADOPTED; highest-priority verification item). [[TSLA]] — AI5 re-priced from a car part to an Optimus part ("INITIAL DEPLOYMENT… FOCUSED ON OPTIMUS ROBOTS RATHER THAN TESLA EVs"), with JPM modelling $450/600mn at GUC vs "CUSTOMER FORECASTS 2-3X OF THESE NUMBERS," plus AI6 foundry diversification to Samsung/TeraFab. [[VRT]] — the HVDC transition finally has vendor-confirmed dates from BOTH Taiwanese leaders (Lite-On HVDC racks 4Q26 → MP 1Q27 for an ASIC program, NVDA 800VDC shipment 2Q27; Delta ±400V for ASICs and 800VDC for VR200 from 4Q26; Google HVDC for ASIC servers "next year"), funded by a Lite-On capex raise to NT$18BN FROM NT$13BN with ~ONE-THIRD EARMARKED FOR HVDC RACKS and Delta to NT$70bn from NT$46bn — ⚠️ credible competitors adding capacity into Vertiv's transition, not just demand validation. [[BE]] — a developer's candid verdict: likes the systems and "BE GETS TAX CREDIT AND BE SOLVES AIR PERMIT CONCERNS" with recips now "BUMPING AGAINST $3000/kW so CLOSE TO BE COST," but "THEY DON'T RAMP FAST OR REACT FAST… NOT AT SCALE," design pattern is fuel cells as baseload with recips load-following (a "40 GW PROJECT (20 GW OF BE FUEL CELLS)"), and ⚠️ "2027 TIMEFRAME FOR ANYTHING DOESN'T SEEM REAL… 2028 SEEMS MORE LEGITIMATE" with hyperscalers "FLAKY, WISHY WASHY, AND HAVE GHOSTED THEM." [[GEV]] — Jefferies to STREET HIGH, +36% vs Street by FY30, PT $1,210 held, while conceding "TURBINE ASPs ARE CRESTING IN NEXT YEAR" and that the 30GW gas OE capacity expansion "INTENSIFIES MID-2030s OVERSUPPLY CONCERNS." A NEW COST LINE ON THE WHOLE BUILD — four US states rolled back or paused data-centre sales-tax exemptions this summer with nine more considering it, worth "7% OR MORE TO EQUIPMENT COSTS" (~$3bn/GW on a ~$40bn IT bill), with TEXAS the biggest exposure (Abbott directed repeal for the 2027 session) and Virginia's electricity-consumption tax the template to watch. [[META]] — ⚠️ THESIS DRIFT within Redburn three days apart: capex path from "$142bn 2027 → $131bn 2028" to "~$145bn in BOTH 2026 and 2027, declining toward $100BN IN 2028" (old values moved to the page's `## Changelog`), $1,000 PT unchanged = 17x on ~$60 of 2028 EPS vs Street ~$40, against a buy-side frame of "low-$30s on 2027 EPS with a 15-17X multiple" and the pecking order "AMZN > GOOGL > META… the market seems LESS WILLING TO WAIT AROUND for the payoff." [[NXPI]] — FT reports talks to buy AMBARELLA (+26% on the week), completing a four-deal analog/edge-AI consolidation pattern with TXN/SLAB, ON/SYNA and MCHP/Hailo. [[AAPL]] — worst EPS move in >12 years, FQ4 guide below on SUPPLY constraints across iPhone/Mac/iPad, ⚠️ but the Services miss and ex-tariff margin weakness are not supply items. [[ARM]] — royalty growth guided to LOW TEENS in Sept from 22% in June, "LED ENTIRELY BY WEAKNESS IN SMARTPHONE ROYALTIES," against a data-centre leg that got firmer the same week. [[QCOM]] — the Apple share loss is now dated to the DEC QUARTER. [[RDDT]] — DAU miss + no new data-licensing agreements + a poor call on Google changes, inside "BY FAR THE WORST INTERNET + MEDIA REPORTING SEASON IN OUR DATA SET GOING BACK 12 YEARS." [[AAOI]] — a falsifiable pre-print bar: capacity from ~100K modules/month in Q1 to >650K by year-end and ~930K after, against management's "the ACTUAL DEMAND IS NOT $1.1B, IT IS $1.4-1.5B." [[OPENAI]] — July ARR alone exceeds the entirety of 2Q revenue, alongside a read that the "2026 VIBE SHIFT is OpenAI actually looking to be THE OPEN PLATFORM… intelligence on tap as a UTILITY," and @chamath dating the tokenmaxxing resolution to "BEFORE EOY 2026" via three named paths. [[SAMSUNG]] — Roundhill's DRAM ETF took CXMT to a 2.47% weight by selling ~3mn Samsung shares, a small but mechanical non-fundamental seller that recurs at each rebalance.**

- **2026-07-25 · /wiki-ingest (daily, full scope) · sources: 18 Outlook notes read in full (from 574 pulled / 166 TMT-screened), 0 new tweets (Twitter feed out of credits — corpus max is 2026-07-24T12:03 UTC), 0 new transcripts · 31 company pages + 4 theme dossiers touched.** Dominated by **UBS's earnings-preview block** (Tim Arcuri / Francois Bouvignies / David Vogt / Karl Keirstead, relayed in Robert Ruple's "Tech Musings" sector-sales weekly) into a 170-company reporting week, plus the Intel Q2 aftermath. **PT drift logged (7 names):** KLAC **$255→$240** (the only cut in semicaps — a multiple call at ~28x from ~30x, while C28E EPS goes *up* to $8.61); LRCX **$200→$435** (a stale mark finally marked to the cycle; C28E EPS $16.47, ~$18.50 at $300B WFE); TER **$440→$500** (C26/27/28E EPS $7.79/$13.27/$18.89); ARM **$470→$360** (PEG 2.4x→1.8x, *entirely* handsets — DC forecasts untouched, C2030 AGI revenue actually raised to ~$14B); QCOM **$235→$190** (C27 rev/EPS $44.8B/$11.31 from $48.6B/$13.03); NXPI **$276→$305**; and AMD gets a new page-high at **UBS $730**. Also: OpenAI's planned through-2030 compute spend **~$600bn → ~$750bn** (WSJ), and UBS raises AMZN **Trn3 units to ~1.8m/~2.8m C26/27 from ~1.7m/2.3m**. **Three cross-cutting threads worth the reader's time.** **(1) A semicap PRICING HIERARCHY finally got ranked** — DB and UBS both say the post-ASML pricing lever is not group-wide: **ASML > AMAT > LRCX > KLAC**, with KLAC named "the least likely in the group to pursue pricing." Filed to [semicap-wfe]. **(2) Memory-cost inflation is now visibly BIFURCATING the handset market** — UBS uses one mechanism (Apple/Samsung absorbing it, Chinese Android not) to cut QCOM *and* ARM and to quantify Apple's BOM risk: a $1,000 iPhone was **BOM ~$580 / memory ~$60 (~10%)** at ~42% contribution margin; **at +300% memory that goes to ~30% of BOM and 24% margin**, with a **$100 price rise recovering only to 31%** — the direct numerical counter to MS/Woodring's 07-14 "higher prices beget higher earnings." Filed to [hbm-memory]. **(3) A POLICY bear case on memory pricing that no bottoms-up model here carries** — Susquehanna's Hosseini: >50% of Korean memory revenue is US-customer, CXMT/YMTC are barred and on the 1260H list (YMTC also Entity List), **and SIG sizes them at 17%/25% of DRAM/NAND supply by YE28**; since **US greenfield fabs can't come online before 2029**, unlocking Chinese supply is the only fast remedy to tightness. **Single most consequential company datapoint: KeyBanc says Intel's EMIB-T yields "have reached 98%"** — which would clear the 95% bar Google set for TPU V9 and flatly contradicts Fubon's 60-70%; the constraint moves to **substrates at 60-70% yield**. The INTC advanced-packaging Signal row was re-marked ⚠ → ⚠→✓ tilting (logged as a contradiction to resolve, not a fact — it is second-hand via a trade newsletter). Other items: Intel GAAP **net loss $10.848bn**, Foundry's **10th consecutive loss / ~$28bn cumulative since 1Q24**, external foundry **$293m Q2 / $467m 1H** vs $22m/$53m a year ago, and a claimed design-win list including **AAPL on 18A *and* 14A for low-end M-series**; Google's **"Frozen v2"** specified — Gemini hardwired into silicon, **6-10x tokens/watt vs latest TPUs**, C28, possibly a **MRVL** design, but "more experimental," sub-TPU volumes, architecture-locked; **Chipstrat's counterweight — TSMC capex intensity 34-36% of revenue vs 53% in the pandemic supercycle**, ~$30bn/yr below its own precedent, and **C.C. Wei on A14 as "an even larger and long-lasting node… than N2"**; **Irrational Analysis on InP** — "80% of InP already goes to AI vs ~25% of DRAM, and InP has no consumer allocation to raid," plus an unverified claim that **"Lumentum cut everyone except Nvidia off"** and ended the Ayar Labs relationship (logged as a claim to confirm; author is long LITE/AXTI/AAOI); **BofA on power** — 158GW of announced behind-the-meter projects but **only 30-50% expected to get through** against a 100GW gap, NARUC regulators shifting to "show me they're real," and turbine demand of **115-120GW/yr in 2026-28 vs ~102GW of capacity**; **GOOGL post-print positioning** — a "buyers strike" with **2027 buy-side EPS still clustered $15.50-16.00 at ~20x** but **2028 "all over the map (some as low as $14)" on the sustainability of Anthropic spend**, i.e. a customer-concentration debate dressed as a capex one. **Positioning flag:** UBS's desk has **NVDA and AVGO short-biased** while the entire semicap and memory complex is long-biased — close to the inverse of the fundamental flow on these pages. **Two sourcing limitations recorded:** the Twitter/X leg contributed nothing (feed out of credits since 07-24 12:03 UTC), and both Michael Burry pieces plus the Chipstrat capex section were paywalled past the point of use. Rebuilt: build_intraquarter, build_wiki_html, build_timelines, refresh_features.

- **2026-07-26 · /wiki-ingest (daily, full scope) · sources: 40 Outlook notes read in full (from 195 pulled / 187 in-window / ~50 TMT-screened), 0 new tweets (Twitter feed still out of credits — corpus max is 2026-07-24T12:03 UTC, unchanged since yesterday), 0 new transcripts, 0 inbox drops · 32 company pages + 9 theme dossiers touched.** A Sunday weekend batch landing into **the busiest earnings week of the quarter by market cap** (MSFT/META/QCOM/ARM/LRCX Wed 7/29; AAPL/AMZN Thu 7/30; KLAC/NXPI/STX/TER/SK hynix Tue 7/28; Samsung Wed 7/29; Kioxia + CXMT IPO). **Three headline events, all reported over the weekend and all logged with their status attached rather than as facts.** **(1) SAMSUNG–AVGO, $200bn / 5 years, covering memory + foundry + advanced packaging** (Reuters 07-25). This is the most consequential single datapoint added to the wiki this quarter, because it is **a foundry win** — the standing bear on [[SAMSUNG]] has been precisely that Samsung Foundry cannot land a marquee leading-edge logic customer at scale, and Broadcom is the largest merchant custom-ASIC house there is. It also bundles all three Samsung AI legs into one counterparty, which is the "turnkey" pitch against TSMC-plus-separate-OSAT, now with a reference customer, and at ~$40bn/yr it forces the capex line materially higher if it converts (a WFE read-through to [[KLAC]]/[[LRCX]]/[[AMAT]]/[[ASML]]). **Explicitly not treated as booked: it is a reported partnership value, not backlog; no node/product/phasing split; BofA's own desk hedged it as "AVGO *possibly* doing $200B deal."** Samsung reports 7/29 — first chance to size or qualify it. **(2) NVDA in talks to provide a $250bn backstop for OpenAI's 10GW Ohio project, leased from SoftBank, ~$500bn all-in including chips** (WSJ). The GPU-debt-backstop mechanism already on file from SemiAnalysis's "AI Project Trinity" (07-06), scaled to one site and one tenant at roughly an order of magnitude above anything previously disclosed. **Status: talks; unconfirmed; NOT added to [[OPENAI]]'s compute-obligations table.** JPM's framing is the one carried: a *test* of the tape, not a catalyst — "if another $250bn guarantee isn't enough, then well… you really have to wonder what is," with the SOX already -19% off its June high. **(3) GOOG disclosed guarantees on as much as $44bn of data-centre lease payments on properties it does not own — up from $6.5bn at end-September, a ~6.8x step-up in three quarters — plus equity warrants in some developer deals** (The Information). Per the reporter's attribution (not Google's), incurred to "supply Anthropic and others" with TPUs. First hard number behind the "bridge capacity" structure, and it sits **off balance sheet, absent from capex, and pointed at the same [[ANTHROPIC]] concentration the 2028-EPS bear case turns on**. **Four cross-cutting threads worth the reader's time.** **(a) The buy-side bogeys DISAGREE materially between desks polling the same investors in the same week, and the spreads are now tabled on [themes/hyperscaler-capex] rather than averaged:** MSFT FY27 capex — UBS $240-250B (research est. $261B; "investor bogey $255-260B") vs **BofA poll $243.5B**; META FY27 — UBS $225-250B vs **BofA $204.2B**; AMZN F27 — UBS **$300-350B** vs BofA **$277.7B**. BofA's META set is the only one implying a **capex peak** (FY29 $235.8B < FY28 $248.6B), and **Redburn's META path of ~$145bn in BOTH '26 and '27 falling toward ~$100bn in '28 is the low outlier against every other mark** — and is what underpins its **new $1,000 META PT** (17x ~$60 of 2028 EPS vs Street ~$40, on a "one prompt entrepreneur" SMB-LLM thesis worth ~$80bn of revenue by 2030). Filed to [_meta/edge]. **(b) The token-cost debate finally has a same-benchmark number, and then a rebuttal that may collapse most of it.** JPM published the all-in cost to run the Artificial Analysis Intelligence Index — **Fable 5 $5,631 · Opus 4.8 $3,753 · Kimi-K3 $2,710 · qwen3.7-plus $311 · deepseek-v4-flash $74** (so Fable 5 is ~2.1x Kimi-K3, ~18x qwen, ~76x deepseek-flash) — and, contrary to consensus, found on Epoch AI's ECI that **the top-3 open vs top-3 closed performance gap has been "relatively stable… rather than materially converging"** (cheaper, not catching up). Same day, **Redburn's Haissl measured his OWN Claude Code usage at ~98% stored-cache reads / <2% fresh input** and concluded open-weight models are **"only 2x cheaper when you adjust for cache."** Note the reconciliation: **JPM's own Fable-5-vs-Kimi ratio is ~2.1x ≈ Redburn's "2x"** — the two houses may agree on the frontier-vs-Kimi gap, leaving the real dispute over the *cheap tail*. If so, the frontier labs' exposure is not Kimi-class models but the very cheap tier absorbing high-volume simple work. **(c) Advanced packaging got its first proper stress test, and the yield picture got WORSE-defined — recorded as such.** UBS sizes the **advanced-packaging TAM at US$19bn (2026E) → US$122bn (2030E), a 59% CAGR**, and even in its stated bull case for Intel (**13% share / US$16bn by 2030 / ~24mn accelerator dies**) TSMC still compounds at a **50% CAGR with 65-70% share and 18% of 2030 sales** — a base-vs-bear spread of only ~2pts of TSMC revenue mix. But UBS's **~90-92% EMIB-T "validation" yield and ~50% EMIB-T substrate yield** now join **Fubon 60-70%, KeyBanc "98%" and an ex-Intel CVP's "98-99% mature"** — a **60-to-99% span on the single gate Google set at 95%+ for TPU v9. Not resolved; do not pick one.** What every source agrees on is that **the constraint has moved from bridges to SUBSTRATES**, and UBS routes around it via **Ibiden capacity, not yield**. Related and new: **glass-core substrates now have dated ramps — Lens Tech + Intel (pilot line end-2026, volume 2H27) vs Innolux + TSMC + Ibiden (MP 2H28-2029)** — the same ~12-18-month ordering as EMIB-T vs CoPoS, i.e. on both bridges and substrates Intel is earlier and TSMC is betting on the later, higher-performance version. **(d) The memory-supply number now has a BULL's estimate that matches the bear's: Nomura initiated CXMT at Buy (TP CNY116) with global DRAM share going ~10% → ~18% by end-2028F, independently corroborating Susquehanna's 17%-by-YE28 from 07-25.** The same note carries the tightest bull case on file — **>7x global memory usage over 2026-30F (60%+ bit CAGR) EVEN AFTER discounting demand 4x for efficiency techniques, against industry bit expansion of only 30-40% CAGR** — and the debut valuation (**15-20x FY27 → ~RMB5T; bull 30x → ~RMB9T** on ~10% share) makes clear the threat is **cost of capital, not technology**. **The single most consequential company datapoint outside the three headline events: NVDA is reported to be cutting the memory spec on NVL72** (after an RTX 50 ramp delay and a SOCAMM cut) because "memory is the majority of the BOM on VR200" — the first *observable* evidence that the memory upcycle this book is long is being paid for out of the accelerator BOM. ⚠️ And the modelling hazard that comes with it: **sell-side HBM inputs span ~$20 to $53/GB for 2027, a ~2.7x spread** — check which is embedded before comparing any two rack models. **Other notable single-name items:** [[QCOM]] reported to be telling customers **prices are up double digits** (Bloomberg) — a direct counter to the memory-pass-through mechanism UBS used to cut its PT to $190 24 hours earlier, and to the ARM royalty cut; **UBS cut MSFT $510 → $480** on multiple while **raising FY27 capex $234B → $261B and FY27 M365 growth +70bps to 16.2%**; **BofA raised MEDIATEK PO NT$5,100 → NT$5,230** and disclosed a **co-developed xAI accelerator ASIC taping out 4Q27** (cloud ASIC US$2.1/13.3/45.8bn '26/'27/'28E), with **MS holding NT$5,588 and expecting the 2027 TPU guide raised to US$12-15bn off a US$100bn TPU TAM** where **AVGO keeps ~80% of revenue share while MediaTek closes the unit gap**; **Waymo reported to be exploring an exit from the [[UBER]] partnership** (FT); **Nokia's AI order intake of €2.8bn vs a €1.3-1.5bn buy-side bogey** — a ~2x beat that the stock did not pay for; **Apple's memory lobbying narrowed to an ex-US carve-out with MU publicly opposed**; Transcripts: 0 new under `E:\ASML - Copia\transcripts`; 0 new in `_equity_calls`.** **34 pages touched: 30 company pages (AMZN, AAPL, RDDT, MEDIATEK, ASML, KIOXIA, SAMSUNG, QCOM, GLW, LRCX, FLEX, ANET, TER, COHR, LITE, TSM, NBIS, DELL, SMCI, INTC, IFX, META, MSFT, GOOG, MU, SKHYNIX, SNDK, STX, AMD, CRWV, AXTI, ADVANTEST, TSLA, SPCX) + 7 themes (hyperscaler-capex, custom-asic-tpu, hbm-memory, semicap-wfe, cowos-packaging, optical-cpo, macro-cycle).** **Headline events: ① [[MEDIATEK]] raised its 2027 ASIC guide on BOTH axes — SAM US$70-80bn → US$80bn and share 10-15% → 15-20%, implying US$12-16bn vs US$7-12bn — with the biggest new guardrail being that the SAM is ACCELERATOR-ONLY (excludes HBM; Google buys HBM directly), so MediaTek ASIC dollars must not be compared gross against AVGO. TPU v9 HVM SLIPPED to early 2028 from late 2027. ② EMIB-T got its first CUSTOMER endorsement — "yes this is true, we are more committed to EMIB-T" — resolving the question MS had flagged as the key open item, with Unimicron's "no full MP before 2027" logged as a compatible TIMING disagreement. ③ [[AMZN]] took six PT raises in 24h and zero cuts (JPM/Barclays to $365, MS $335, BofA $320, JEF $330, Bernstein $320), with the first Street FY27/28 capex marks ($365bn/$445bn JPM vs $325bn/$350bn MS — a ~$40bn spread that IS the memory-pass-through assumption). ④ [[AAPL]] took five cuts and one hold; the Woodring call is the densest source of the run (App Store "problem child," Apple now "two, maybe even three" in TSMC priority, FY27 iPhone pricing +~20% with units −LSD, capex "comically low" at ~$2.5bn/qtr vs R&D +35%, on-B/S inventory DOUBLED q/q and vendor non-trade receivables at a 15-YEAR HIGH but EXCLUDING advanced SoCs — Apple can buy around memory, not around 3nm). ⑤ [[RDDT]] −23%: every house cut its PT or multiple, NOT ONE cut estimates — a duration de-rating, with GOOGL sized at ~40-50% of inbound traffic and a NEW "Google dependency" theme row added. ⑥ [[KIOXIA]] printed a modest miss answered with a ¥800bn buyback + 3-for-1 split, on ASP +70% q/q LIKE-FOR-LIKE and low-single-digit bit growth. ⑦ [[AXTI]] blowout: record revenue, capacity ahead of plan, and [[LITE]] signing an AXT LTA despite a ~7-YEAR SUMITOMO deal — retiring the prior bear case and proving availability (not cost) is the InP constraint.** **Cross-cutting findings promoted to themes: (a) the LTA MECHANICS — Samsung 60-70% coverage with a sub-5% q/q price-cut FLOOR against increases open at 10-20%+ on 5-YEAR ROLLING tenors, i.e. downside collars with uncapped upside, which INVERTS the usual bear reading of high contract coverage (and Kioxia at 50% of CY28 volume); (b) a THREE-PART BASIS GUARDRAIL before any cloud league table holds — GOOGL books TPU system sales GROSS vs AWS's NET Bedrock, TPU carries structurally lower margin, and GCP's headline acceleration flatters on base/comp/mix; (c) the CoWoS OUTSOURCED SHARE quantified at 8%→46%→75%→85%→90% for 2024-28E, meaning TSMC's own capacity figures now UNDERSTATE system capacity; (d) the BEHAVIOURAL LESSON of the week — AMZN raised capex $20bn MORE than expected and rallied 14% while META raised LESS and fell 8%: the market pays for quantified payback, not restraint; (e) 300MM WAFERS called into shortage with SPOT HEADING ABOVE LTA pricing (DB upgrade of Siltronic), a new input-cost constraint; (f) VENDOR-FINANCED CAPACITY as a category — NVDA writing minimum-revenue-guarantee backstops for neoclouds (Firmus, Sharon AI) and GOOGL backstopping an Anthropic 1.6GW/$15bn campus, which decouples reported capacity from independent end-demand; (g) the Situational Awareness liquidation (~$16bn book to Citadel, 4x levered, −67% in July) with record factor moves and a semis L/S ratio back to the 60th %-ile on 12m but STILL the 95th since '18.** **⚠️ Corrections/flags: a bad on-page figure fixed on [[AAPL]] (MS "PT $333.43" was the SHARE PRICE; the real mark was $364 → $360 — struck through in place, not deleted); off-balance-sheet commitment claims (GOOGL ~$826bn SPV, META ~$700bn, $3tn aggregate) logged as UNVERIFIED @zerohedge claims requiring filing confirmation; a MediaTek SerDes generation inconsistency (448G in prepared remarks vs 400G in Q&A) flagged unresolved; BofA's MediaTek-xAI co-development claim logged as an assertion, since management confirmed nothing; and LRCX's ALD-Moly rebuttal of a competitor logged as CONTESTED, not settled.** **Not ingested (noted rather than silently dropped): AIXA (MS PT €45→€40 with FY27/28 EPS +31%/+30% — already fully present on the page from the 07-30 run), and ~70 lower-priority whitelist emails whose bodies were not fetched (mostly duplicate desk relays of items already captured, plus off-coverage Asia names — Realtek, E Ink, Xiaomi, ASE/Hon/Chroma standalone pages do not exist, so those were folded as read-throughs into TSM/TER/ADVANTEST/AMD/TSLA instead of spawning pages).**

- **2026-08-01 (21h, scheduled) · /wiki-ingest — full scope. A Saturday delta: the weekend-research layer of the earnings week, and it is unusually load-bearing because next week is the second wave of prints.** Sources: **Outlook 1,367 emails pulled (3-day window) → 57 after the 2026-07-31 19:00 cutoff and folder filter → 14 bodies fetched in full** (the rest Brazil macro/politics, index/flows admin, or non-TMT). **Twitter/X corpus fresh — max 2026-08-01T12:31 UTC; 373 non-RT posts in the window → 24 tickers tagged, but Saturday yield was thin and only ~8 posts carried substance.** **0 new transcripts under `E:\ASML - Copia\transcripts`; 0 new `_equity_calls`; `_inbox` empty (already swept by /run-inbox on 07-31).** ⚠️ **Duplication check ran first and killed a meaningful share of the candidate set — the 07-31 21h run had already ingested the Chipstrat ASML pricing piece (email 07-31 19:27), the ASE $10.5bn capex raise, the advanced-packaging TAM $19bn→$122bn/59% CAGR, the KeyBanc 18A 85%-from-65% mark, the [[NVDA]]/Amkor $1.5bn prepayment and [[MSFT]]'s RPO-ex-OpenAI disclosure. Those were verified present and NOT re-written.** **30 pages touched: 30 company pages ([[MEDIATEK]], [[ADVANTEST]], [[AMD]], [[ANET]], [[ALAB]], [[WDC]], [[IFX]], [[MCHP]], [[ON]], [[AKAM]], [[PLTR]], [[ANTHROPIC]], [[SAMSUNG]], [[LRCX]], [[AMAT]], [[STX]], [[OPENAI]], [[GLW]], [[SNDK]], [[AIXA]], [[AXTI]], [[TSEM]], [[INTC]], [[TSM]], [[GOOG]], [[QCOM]], [[KIOXIA]], [[SKHYNIX]], [[MU]], [[ARM]]) + 6 theme dossiers (hyperscaler-capex, semicap-wfe, macro-cycle, optical-cpo, hbm-memory, custom-asic-tpu).**

  **Headline findings — ① THE BINDING CONSTRAINT ON 2026-27 WFE SHIPMENTS IS NAMED, AND IT IS NOT DEMAND AND NOT THE OEMs.** UBS/Arcuri, inside a UCTT preview rather than an OEM note: **"major SPE suppliers like [[LRCX]] and [[AMAT]] are set to grow shipments ~10% q/q for the foreseeable future, LIMITED LARGELY BY THE CAPABILITIES OF SUB-SYSTEM SUPPLIERS like UCTT."** This theme has spent a month arguing about the SIZE of the WFE TAM ($150B/$170-175B/$220B+/$250B); the number that gets SHIPPED is now said to be set one tier down. **And it has a date: UCTT's Malaysia site is modelled fully utilised by C4Q26 and its entire ~$4B footprint grown into by C1H27, against a 12-16 month greenfield build-out — "management would effectively need to pull the trigger RIGHT AROUND NOW." UCTT reports 08-03: absence of a greenfield announcement implies a hard ceiling on LRCX/AMAT shipment cadence arriving mid-2027 that no WFE forecast on the wiki contains.** Consensus is ~78% below UBS on UCTT C2027 EPS ($5 vs $8.89). **The pattern repeated FOUR times in one week and is now filed as a reusable principle: Zeiss's Oberkochen expansion caps [[ASML]] EUV output; [[AIXA]] customers are short MOCVD TOOLS with slots reserved into 2027; 300mm wafers heading to shortage with spot above LTA. WFE revenue can under-run WFE demand for several quarters without demand ever weakening — a different risk profile than the cyclical one the multiple prices.**

  **② THE FUNDING SIDE OF ~$750BN CAPEX GETS ITS OWN NUMBER, REVISED UP ~19% IN A WEEK.** UBS Credit (Sachin Ganesh) raised 2026 hyperscaler public debt issuance from **$230-240bn to $275-285bn**, "reflecting expectation for further upside surprises to capex and LIMITED FREE CASH FLOW CAPACITY" — i.e. ~35-40% of the year's hyperscaler capex is bond-funded and the share is rising. ⚠️ **"IG tech credit spreads have started to show signs of FRAGILITY… more tech supply still needs to be absorbed (WHICH SHOULD BE ABLE TO HAPPEN IF PLANS ARE CLEARLY STATED AND THERE IS A PATH TO MONETIZATION)." That conditional is the same test the equity market applied this season — the mechanism behind [[AMZN]] rallying 14% on a bigger raise while [[META]] fell 8% on a smaller one. Equity and credit are converging on one criterion, so a hyperscaler that fails it now faces a higher cost of capital as well as a lower multiple.** Counterweight logged not adopted: Gavin Baker's pinned post says the market is over-reacting to the spread widening.

  **③ [[ADVANTEST]]'s NVIDIA CONCENTRATION IS A DISCLOSED NUMBER FOR THE FIRST TIME — 20.2% OF FY2025 SALES (¥228,273mn), against a prior-year line disclosed as "–" on both amount and percentage.** Ties to the reported ¥1,128.6bn topline. **This converts MS's qualitative "sole share for NVDA GPU testing" into a hard figure and cuts both ways: it sizes the franchise, and it means management's flagged "15-20% GPU-test share" entrant and GS's naming of [[TER]] as challenger threaten ~a fifth of group revenue rather than a diffuse book. The GPU-test-durability debate should be re-weighted upward as P&L risk.**

  **④ MERCHANT-ASIC MARGIN SHAPE STATED FOR THE FIRST TIME — [[MEDIATEK]]: "GM will be DILUTIVE, but OpM will be SIZABLE AND ACCRETIVE… opex ratio will come down substantially."** Resolves how a US$12-16bn low-margin leg coexists with a flat 46%±1.5pt corporate GM guide, and is now the general modelling rule for the custom-ASIC theme. Same MS call note **dated EMIB-T mass production to 2028 with TSMC CoWoS/CoPoS as a named back-up ("switching the technology might take time")**, **resolved the 448G/400G discrepancy in favour of 448G ready 2H27** (the number was the transcription error, not the calendar), disclosed **a pre-validated RACK/system design with one undisclosed customer** — the first evidence against the AlphaSense verdict that MediaTek lacks system-level capability — and had management state **"CSP still requiring HBM… would still be the mainstream in the near term."**

  **⑤ A REPORT SAYS [[GOOG]] MAY DESIGN HBM OUT OF A GEMINI PART ENTIRELY — "Frozen V2 TPU hardwires SRAM onto silicon, DITCHING [[TSM]]'s CoWoS packaging."** If real and if volume, it removes a Gemini-serving part from the CoWoS AND HBM queues at once, and Google — which buys HBM directly — has the strongest incentive of any ASIC buyer to do it. ⚠️ **NOT ADOPTED: single press relay, no confirmation, unverified codename, and it DIRECTLY CONTRADICTS MediaTek management's characterisation of its own customer the day before. Flagged as the highest-priority verification item on GOOG / custom-asic-tpu / cowos-packaging.**

  **⑥ THE SITUATIONAL AWARENESS POST-MORTEM GETS ITS MECHANISM, AND THE THING THAT DIDN'T SELL IS THE MOST INFORMATIVE PART.** Irrational Analysis (30+ contacts): SALP ran **4-5x gross leverage with shorts that "were NOT ANYWHERE CLOSE TO FACTOR NEUTRAL"** — long AI semis, short traditional SaaS, i.e. **anti-correlated shorts that "provided little risk-related benefit and were simply MORE LEVERAGE ON THE SAME FACTOR (AI)."** The reusable lesson: a long-AI-winners/short-AI-losers book is a levered single-factor bet, invisible to any net-exposure risk check. **And per the WSJ, the fund considered selling [[ANTHROPIC]] shares and CHANGED ITS MIND — liquidating its entire listed book instead. Revealed preference on how private AI marks were defended against listed AI beta at the trough, and the reason no distressed Anthropic secondary printed in July. The "$3bn" is the author's inferred cash need, not a quoted block.** Sequencing corroborated the forced-flow read of [[KIOXIA]] (07-27), [[SKHYNIX]]/[[BE]] (07-28 — Bloom's best-ever call faded because the marginal seller was the same fund) and [[SNDK]].

  **⑦ THE NAND SIDE SPLITS THREE WAYS ON QLC AND IT IS A CAPITAL-ALLOCATION FORK, NOT A TIMING DIFFERENCE (Susquehanna/Hosseini).** [[SAMSUNG]] the aggressor — QLC bits in 2H26 to **more than double vs 1H26** on V9 2Tb QLC plus **V10 V-NAND mass production in AUGUST 2026**; [[KIOXIA]] explicitly declining — **"high-capacity QLC solutions remain TOO COSTLY for broad deployment,"** prioritising TLC eSSD on 8th-gen BiCS (9th-gen samples now shipping); [[SKHYNIX]] taking the middle path on node migration (321-layer to 50% of domestic production by year-end). ⚠️ **Direct read-through to [[SNDK]] (reports 08-05): it shares fabs with a partner that has publicly decided QLC is uneconomic. And SIMO's "SSDs reach the density crossover point needed to displace HDDs" in 2027-28 CONTRADICTS the [[STX]]/[[WDC]] framing published the same day ("pace of SSD replacing nearline HDD is slowing"; rising NAND prices widen HDD's cost advantage; HDD the cold-data workhorse "over the next five years"). Logged unresolved — 2Tb-die availability is the arbitrating observable.**

  **⑧ THE 08-03/08-06 PREVIEW WAVE, filed name by name with the bogeys attached (UBS Tech Musings, Ruple + the covering analysts).** **[[AMD]]** (Buy $730, sentiment 9/10 on only 8.3% implied vol — the asymmetry IS the risk): FQ2 $11.33B/$1.65 vs Street $11.30B/$1.61, FQ3 guide $12.71B/$1.91 vs $12.52B/$1.88, **above Street on Data Center in both quarters and BELOW on Client in both**; C28E EPS $27.57 with a ~$30 bull, and Arcuri calls his own C27 conservative — **"only ~$44B for DC GPU revenue in C2027, which would only be 3-3.5GW — VERY LOW given announcements."** **[[ANET]]** (Buy $187): June tracking $2.88bn/$0.93 vs UBSe $2.81bn/$0.88 and Sept ~$3.0bn vs cons $2.94bn, but **FY GM 61.8% vs cons 62.7% and OPM 45% vs 46.5%** — above on revenue, below on both margin lines; ⚠️ **checks say supply has NOT improved, [[AVGO]] silicon lead times still >52 weeks, which CONTESTS MS's 07-30 read that lead times are normalising to 6-8 weeks.** **[[ALAB]]** (first UBS mark: Neutral $400): Trn3 units raised to 1.8mn/2.8mn C26/C27, but ⚠️ **"CAUTION AGAINST APPLYING A ~$1,000/XPU OPPORTUNITY ACROSS EXPECTED Trn3 UNIT VOLUMES"** — which contradicts how the page's own Sinal table reads management's guide — and a new architectural bear leg: **AWS "increasingly interested in migrating away from its proprietary NeuronLink toward NVLink- or UALink-based alternatives," with [[MRVL]] named as the NVLink Fusion beneficiary.** **[[WDC]]** ⚠️ **THESIS DRIFT, PT $375→$560** (page held $145; the $375 rung was never captured — logged as a gap): FQ1 $4.4B/$4.66 vs Street $4.0B/$3.73, yet Neutral held on **"customers will 2X ORDER HDD CAPACITY until demand starts to flatten"** — the direct inverse of MS's "HDDs are not memory" rebuttal set; plus a new supply fact, **WDC/[[STX]]/Toshiba "starting to buy heads externally from TDK,"** which is a route around the "no new capacity for 1-3 years" barrier the structural bull case rests on. **[[IFX]]** ⚠️ **THESIS DRIFT, Buy €44 → NEUTRAL €61** (largely closing the DIVERGES gap flagged 07-27; re-score at next /wiki-edge): FQ4 guide ~€4.6bn = +12% q/q vs ~+5% seasonality but segment margin 23.5% vs cons 24.1%; AI guide €1.5bn 2026 expected raised to €1.5-2.0bn; **new bear leg is 2027 AI SHARE loss to [[ADI]]/[[TXN]] on internal capacity constraints, plus China auto −23% YTD and NEV −19% YTD.** **[[MCHP]]** ⚠️ **THESIS DRIFT, Buy $85 → $130**: Sept guide UBSe $1.609bn/$0.89 vs cons $1.55bn/$0.80 (~11% above on EPS), "among the LAST analog peers to raise pricing," 65% GM target hit on exit from FY26; **two corporate actions — the HAILO edge-AI tuck-in and COO RICH SIMONCIC RESIGNING 08-17, the latter logged as an open execution question mid-way through the 9-point plan that IS the bull case.** **[[ON]]** (Neutral $95, lowest sentiment at 6/10 = the setup argument): Data Center revenue to DOUBLE in Q2, but ⚠️ **"ON continued to LOSE PRICING SHARE WHILE BUILDING UNIT INVENTORIES during Q2"** — share-and-inventory deterioration inside a recovering market — and auto evidence conflicts (UBS's own distributor checks "mixed" vs [[ADI]]'s "bullish surprise" and [[TXN]]'s mid-teens q/q). **[[AKAM]]** (first UBS mark: Neutral $125): 2Q CIS growth CUT to 40% from 43% and the ramp called **"more 4Q weighted"** against consensus at 40/47/70% — a shape disagreement, not a conversion one — with the **$1.8bn 7-year contract counterparty stated as [[ANTHROPIC]]** and a new cost flag (RTX Pro 6000 retail +55% in June). **[[PLTR]]** (Buy $200 held): −15.7% since FQ1, −32% YTD; ⚠️ **the objection is structural, "investor wariness to own into WHAT IS LIKELY THE PEAK GROWTH QUARTER"**; US Commercial ~$716M is the deciding metric; and Keirstead softens on the moat — **"still strong but ADMITTEDLY HARD TO DEFEND given pace of model improvements."**

  **⑨ Foundry/OSAT recap (Semiecosystem/LaPedus) added the yield triangle from a named analyst and a fab that was not on the wiki: KeyBanc's John Vinh — [[INTC]] 18A 85% (from 65%), [[TSM]] N2 90%, [[SAMSUNG]] SF2 50-60%.** ⚠️ **Read the derivative: TSMC's lead over Intel narrowed from 25pts to 5pts in a quarter while the gap to Samsung (30-40pts) did not — Intel is becoming a real second source on yield, Samsung is not yet, which is exactly consistent with MediaTek committing to EMIB-T while naming TSMC as back-up.** **Net-new: Samsung will BREAK GROUND ON A SECOND 300MM TAYLOR, TEXAS FAB BY YEAR-END, ramping 2030** — the foundry arm committing 2030 capital while the memory arm guides no meaningful supply increase through 2028. Samsung's own foundry line — **"improved significantly PRIOR TO INCENTIVE-RELATED PROVISIONS, driven by HBM BASE-DIE DEMAND and strong US orders"** — is pre-provision and HBM-base-die-driven, further corroborating JPM's 90-95% HBM / 5-10% foundry decomposition of the [[AVGO]] MoU rather than the "marquee leading-edge win" framing.

  **⑩ Smaller but filed: [[TSM]] ~10% price increases across select 12/16/28nm** (press-relayed) — the first TRAILING-edge evidence for a pricing thesis built almost entirely on leading-edge, and higher-quality precisely because mature nodes are competitive; **[[TSEM]]'s $1.3bn SiPho order book validated by a competitor's analyst**, who simultaneously models GFS reaching $1.2-1.3bn SiPho revenue by C2028 — i.e. the market is not being modelled as a Tower monopoly; **[[QCOM]] CEO claims the company has "kind of replaced" Apple with the data centre** (an invited comparison the Street will now hold them to; UBS has QCOM one-way SHORT-biased); **[[ARM]] appears in ALL THREE UBS positioning buckets simultaneously** — the only name to do so — which is the market pricing Hosseini's "DC AI Shines, Though SP Weakens Again" split literally; **[[GLW]] — Hosseini's "Springboard's Success Has Become a Source of Complacency"** names the mechanism behind a >15% drop on a beat-every-line quarter, and generalises with [[KLAC]] into the season's rule that meeting a published plan now reads as a guide-down; **[[AXTI]]** gets a non-fundamental explanation for its early de-rating (China banning total return swaps → forced Asian deleveraging), ⚠️ unverified and from a self-declared long, needing an independent check on the rule change; **CCRAFT**, a US$7.8M TFLN photonics foundry seed round with a European AI-infrastructure operator participating — far too small to matter commercially, logged only because it is the first capital forming around a THIRD material platform positioned against both silicon photonics and InP, which is the substitution risk under the whole [[AXTI]]/[[LITE]]/[[COHR]] scarcity thesis; and **[[OPENAI]] cutting prices on smaller models "in response to token maxing" WHILE reaching ~$47bn ARR and >1bn users** — the first time the company's own pricing action lands on one side of the tokenmaxxing debate, plus the containment breach widening from Hugging Face to five platforms.

  ⚠️ **Not ingested, noted rather than silently dropped:** the GS Pasquariello "AI earnings and the key debates" note (a podcast link with no transcribed substance), the GS Weekend Macro Call and "Thoughts from the Floor" (macro-dominant, no TMT single-name content beyond what is already logged), The Information Weekend's "Why Apple Should Buy Granola" (speculative op-ed, no reported facts), the Chase card-spending relay (598-byte teaser, no data), Thomas Chong's QM weekly (478 bytes, China internet, off-coverage), and ~43 lower-priority window emails that were Brazil macro/politics, index/flow admin or non-TMT. **Off-coverage names surfaced but deliberately NOT given pages per the whitelist rule — ASE, Amkor, X-Fab, CCRAFT, UCTT, ENTG, GFS, SIMO, IQE, Cohu, Chroma, Hon Precision — were folded as read-throughs into [[TSM]], [[LRCX]], [[AMAT]], [[TSEM]], [[ADVANTEST]], [[AXTI]] and the relevant themes instead.** **Known gap carried forward: the [[WDC]] UBS PT history is missing the $145→$375 rung.** Cursor: 30 tickers + `_default` advanced to **2026-08-01**.

- ⚠️ **2026-08-03 — CY2026 consensus defect found and NOT auto-fixed (needs a human call).** `fetch_estimates.py` sums each CY from quarters and re-pulls already-reported quarters via `BEST_FPERIOD_OVERRIDE`, which returns the **pre-print consensus mean, not the comparable actual** — so **every CY2026 line understates a beat**, proportional to the beat. Verified by live probe: TER **-$179.3M / -$0.88 (-3.5% / -9.8%)**, KLAC **-$101.7M (-0.65% / -1.6%)**. **Affects all 97 names** (every one has ≥1 reported 2026 quarter: 53 with one, 44 with two); **CY2027 is clean** (`n_actual = 0` for all 97), so most of the edge tracker is unaffected. Interim rule: **for CY2026 comparisons use the sum of the quarterly lines, not the CY2026 aggregate.** Full write-up in `reconciliation-2026-08-01.md` item ⑤ + method flag 7.

The model business is written off and the cloud business re-rated in the same note: "for all intents and purposes, DEEPMIND IS NO LONGER A FRONTIER LAB… their odds of reaching SOTA again HAVE DROPPED TO ZERO," versus "the biggest beneficiary is NEITHER [[ANTHROPIC]] NOR [[OPENAI]] — IT'S GOOGLE CLOUD… IT'S NOW CLEAR THAT THOMAS KURIAN WON."** 🔴 **THE HARD FALSIFIABLE NUMBER: Gemini first-party API token growth decelerated from +60% q/q in 1Q26 (10B → 16B tokens/minute) to +38% in 2Q26 (→ 22B).** ✅ **Two items on the wiki got UPGRADED by it: the "Discovery Loop" four-person exodus (Dean + Ghemawat + Le + Vinyals), flagged 08-06 as an unverified press relay because The Information named only Dean, is now CORROBORATED by a second independent source; and the CNBC TPU-allocation grievance is now SIZED — ">20% OF TOTAL TPU SHIPMENTS FROM 3Q26 TO 4Q27 ARE BEING SOLD DIRECTLY TO ANTHROPIC," excluding what GCP already rents.** ⚠️⚠️ **AND THE MOST IMMEDIATELY USABLE THING IN THE WHOLE RUN IS AN ACCOUNTING TRAP: GCP now sells FULL TPU SYSTEMS TO EXTERNAL SPVs, booked ON A GROSS BASIS AT ~$35B/GW. At ~$1.2B in 2Q26 that means "CORE GCP GREW IN THE LOW 70s" against the 82% headline — roughly 10 POINTS OF REPORTED GCP GROWTH IS HARDWARE, NOT CLOUD, at low-30s EBIT vs mid-to-high-30s for GCP overall. DO NOT COMPARE HEADLINE GCP GROWTH TO AWS/AZURE WITHOUT STRIPPING IT. This is the THIRD instance of the same error class on this wiki after [[CRWV]] capex (placed-in-service, not cash) and the estimates.json CY-sum defect — filed together as one recurring failure mode.** ⚠️ Flagged unverified: the claim that **Gemini 3.5 Pro was SILENTLY CANCELLED** and that Gemini now ranks "8th or 9th."
  🔴🔴 **[[SPCX]] / [[MSFT]] — "SPACEX 10GW IN 2027" (08-07). The first published attempt to VERIFY the 10GW number rather than react to it: "we believe THE NUMBER IS REAL… on track to build ABOUT 10GW BY YEAR-END 2027" against "$300-500B IN CAPEX IN 2027, ON PAR WITH [[AMZN]] AND [[GOOG]]." Financing answered in two legs — [[NVDA]] VENDOR FINANCING ("this is LIKELY WHY ELON DECLARED TO BE NVIDIA EXCLUSIVE"; xAI/SpaceX "have ACTIVELY EVALUATED ALTERNATIVES LIKE TPU AND [[AMD]]") plus pricing at 30-50M/MW/year that "PAYS BACK THE CAPEX IN LESS THAN A YEAR."** 🔴 **AND THE OFFTAKER IS NAMED: MICROSOFT. "MSFT HAS CONTRACTED OVER 10GW [YTD]… ~$300B IN NEW BINDING COMMITMENTS," with claimed impact "AZURE ACCELERATING REVENUE GROWTH FROM ~42% TO OVER 100% BY NEXT YEAR."** ⚠️ **NOT ADOPTED — single source, proprietary model. But the MECHANISM is checkable and is a revenue-per-MW argument, not a demand argument: the Oct-2025 $250B [[OPENAI]] deal (~7GW) left Microsoft unable to serve Foundry or Copilot, "the services that come at the HIGHEST MARGIN AND REVENUE PER MW, BY FAR" — OpenAI capacity earns ~$14M/MW/year against a modelled ~$100M/MW/year.** ⚠️ **HIGHEST-PRIORITY VERIFICATION ITEM FROM THIS RUN: "THE DEAL REWORKED IN APRIL 2026 DROPPED THE OLD 20% REVENUE SHARE FROM THE EQUATION." Check it against the April 2026 disclosure — if true it reframes hyperscaler capex from "spending to keep up" to "spending to RE-MIX."** ⚠️ **The load-bearing input under both — "$100B/GW/year of inference revenue vs ~$12B/GW/year of cost at $3/GPU-hr" — is a proprietary simulator output and is NOT adopted. Counterweight from 22V the same day: permitted power is the constraint, "THE PERMITTING PATH IS GETTING HARDER, NOT EASIER," with TX Governor Abbott directing the PUCT to AUDIT ALL DATA-CENTER POWER APPLICATIONS.** ⚠️ Unit note: SemiAnalysis's "30-50M/MW/year" and management's "$30-50 PER WATT" are **arithmetically identical — one datapoint, two framings**; and the note's headline "$500B ARR" does not reconcile with its own body ("$300B ARR… assumes ONLY 50% of 2027 incremental compute is monetized"). Carry $300B, flag $500B.

  **THE PRINT WAVE, DAY TWO — and the pattern across all three is that ESTIMATES WENT UP WHILE PRICE TARGETS AND RATINGS DID NOT FOLLOW.**
  🔴 **[[MCHP]] — six houses and TWO management calls in 24 hours, and the two houses that cut are the ones worth reading. JPM OW $120 (rolled to Dec-27) · BofA Buy PO $100 + hosted CEO Sanghi/CFO Bjornholt/IR Daudi · GS Buy $115 · Barclays EW $105 unchanged · ⚠️ MS EW $94 → $103 (multiple CUT 28x → 25x, "we view the NT GM% as PEAK") · ⚠️ UBS Buy $130 → $120 · ⚠️ Jefferies BUY $120 → $100.** **Jefferies RAISED Cal-26 EPS to $3.21 from $2.92 and cut the PT anyway: "upturns typically last EIGHT [quarters]" and September is the sixth. UBS cut C2028 revenue AND EPS because "BOTH THE DISTRIBUTORS AND DIRECT BUYERS NOW SEEM TO BE BUILDING INVENTORY ON FEARS OF SHORTAGES" — the most bullish pre-print house naming double-ordering.** ✅ **Both 08-01 bogeys resolved WITH ASTERISKS: the CY26 $500MM data-center target is superseded by ~$1B, but partly by RE-DEFINITION (UBS documents MCHP sweeping an extra ~7% of C2025 revenue into DC; the ~$1B = DC Solutions BU ~$500M + catalog DC ~$500M), and Jefferies flags PCIe wins "SKEW TOWARD SMALLER DESIGNS, OUTSIDE OF ONE LARGE WIN"; and the 65% GM ceiling is cleared but management CAPPED the slope — the SepQ 66.5% contains a lumpy 100%-GM licensing quarter (sized for the first time at ~$40mn) and a one-time disty revaluation, pricing is "ONE AND DONE," and mgmt "DOES NOT INTEND TO RECREATE THE POST-COVID PLAYBOOK."** 🔴 **Three net-new facts: the entire +38% y/y June quarter is VOLUME ("recent growth has been ENTIRELY UNIT-DRIVEN as pricing increases only begin taking effect this month and next"); capital allocation is now "ALL AVAILABLE CASH… TO PAY DOWN DEBT… NO PLANS FOR BUYBACKS OR DIVIDEND INCREASES"; and HALO is re-framed from "immaterial" to "ACCELERATES MCHP's EDGE AI ROADMAP BY ROUGHLY FIVE YEARS."** ⚠️ COO succession after 08-17 remains unaddressed by all six houses and both management calls.
  ✅ **[[NET]] — all three lines flagged unverified yesterday are now filled, and the important one is favourable: GROSS MARGIN 73.1%, IMPROVED SEQUENTIALLY FOR THE FIRST TIME IN EIGHT QUARTERS ("beginning to stabilize"), which resolves the standing mix-trade bear. PF op income $96M / 13.8% vs cons $91M / 13.6%.** ⚠️ **DBNR STILL MISSING — no house carried it; Barclays' 118% → ~119% pool-of-funds call is neither confirmed nor failed.** ⚠️ **PTs: Barclays OW $300 → $355; JPM NEUTRAL $145 (Dec-26) → $350 (Dec-27) — a +141% target increase with NO rating change, purely on valuation ("rarified territory"). JPM's framing is the one to keep: NET gets the AI tailwind "WITHOUT THE HURDLES IN RELATION TO EITHER CUSTOMER CONCENTRATION OR CAPITAL INTENSITY that have been a concern for some other companies in our coverage" — an explicit contrast with [[AKAM]], printing the same night.** 🔴 **Act 4 gets a named counterparty: a RESEARCH PILOT WITH [[OPENAI]]. Workers added nearly 2mn developers in Q2 alone vs 1.5mn in ALL of 2025.**
  ✅ **[[AKAM]] — every unverified line closed, and management disclosed CIS UNIT ECONOMICS for the first time: "cash gross margins MID-60s% to MID-70s%, PF operating margins LOW-to-MID-20s% to LOW-30s%, with a 6-9 MONTH window between deal signing and revenue recognition."** ⚠️ **Filed with the caveat that the LOW end is roughly where AKAM's corporate margins already sit — CIS is only accretive at the TOP of the disclosed range.** **Capex Q2 $346.5M (32% of revenue, below the ~40-41% guide on a GPU delivery slip) → Q3 guided $475-525M ≈ 45%; cash GM 71% vs 70.5% guide; PF op margin 24.6% vs 25.5% guide, Q3 guided BELOW consensus.** 🔴 **THE BUYBACK IS PAUSED to fund CIS, which retro-explains the "increased shares outstanding" behind yesterday's FY EPS tweak. JPM raised its target 32% ($100 → $132) and STAYED UNDERWEIGHT.** ⚠️ JPM names the new risk: management is "SETTING A HIGH BAR FOR THEMSELVES by continuing the cadence of large deal announcements CONCURRENT WITH EARNINGS RELEASES" — three straight quarters headlined by a mega-deal.

  **MEMORY — THE 12-Hi QUESTION IS CLOSED AND THE FIRST CREDIBLE LTA BEAR ARRIVES FROM A CYCLE BULL.**
  🔴 **SUPERSEDED: the Rubin Ultra HBM configuration. Yesterday's GFHK line ("8-Hi finalized, YET 12-Hi REMAINS ON LINE-UP") is retired. UBS: "NVIDIA WILL LIKELY INITIALLY USE HBM4 8-Hi FOR RUBIN ULTRA FOLLOWED BY A SWITCH TO HBM4E 8-Hi… compared to our PRIOR EXPECTATION OF HBM4E 12-Hi… IN RESPONSE TO PERSISTENT TIGHT SUPPLY" — 384GB → 512GB per GPU versus a 768GB prior estimate, a ~33% content cut. DIGITIMES corroborates the direction the same day.** ✅ **AND THE DE-SPEC IS BULLISH ON BITS: UBS RAISED 2027 [[NVDA]] HBM end-consumption to 25.1bn Gb (from 24.0bn) "AS GPU UNITS IMPLIED GO UP TO 12.6MIL FROM 11.0MIL." Less memory per die, more dies — a bill of materials rationed by SUPPLY. Industry HBM 61.5bn Gb in '27, +91% y/y; blended HBM ASP +79% y/y; DRAM bit demand +40.1% vs supply +20.6%, "PROLONGED DRAM UNDERSUPPLY THROUGH AT LEAST 2Q28"; DRAM upcycle runs longer than NAND (2Q28E vs 4Q27E).** ⚠️ **UBS nonetheless CUT '27E memory industry revenue 9% to US$1.61tn because "LTAs ARE REIGNING IN SOME OF THE PRICING UPSIDE," compensated by LTAs being "OPM/ROE ACCRETIVE THROUGH THE CYCLE." Memory stocks −36% from the June peak, "now discount PRE-AI THROUGH-CYCLE ROEs." Samsung is UBS's Key Call Buy; CXMT initiated Buy with the China threat called overstated (9% bit share '27; "market expectations are PROBABLY MORE ALIGNED WITH EQUIPMENT DELIVERY VS ACTUAL INSTALLATION/VALIDATION — and hence too high"; G5 sub-15nm late '26 vs the top-3 at sub-11nm in 2H27, yields "NOT AT PAR").** 🔴 **THE BEAR, AND IT IS THE FIRST CREDIBLE ONE ON LTAs: Jefferies — LTAs "EXTENUATE A CYCLE RATHER THAN PREVENT IT"; "DEALS SIGNED AT 80% GROSS MARGIN IMPLY SOFTER ASPs THAN OPEN MARKET"; and, read off the equipment layer, "[[LRCX]]'s RECORD NAND REVENUE indicates SOMEONE'S ADDING CAPACITY IN NAND, LIKELY THE KOREANS, EARLIER THAN PREVIOUSLY ANTICIPATED." Bernstein's tracker agrees on direction: 3QCY26 contract increases "NARROWED NOTABLY VS 2Q AND MAY BE A BIT BELOW THE LATEST STREET EXPECTATION… ROOM FOR UPSIDE IS LIKELY LIMITED, GIVEN MEMORY IS BECOMING A BURDEN FOR BOTH AI & NON-AI APPLICATIONS & PRICE CAPS SET IN SOME LTAs."** ✅ **Counterweight on the "it's all price" bear: CQ2 memory UNITS +32.2% q/q (UBS/SIA) — "NOT PURELY AN ASP PHENOMENON."** **[[SKHYNIX]] committed ~US$38-39bn to Yongin Y2 + M17 — ground-break July 2027, FIRST CLEANROOM JUNE 2029, WFE 2031 — i.e. NO relief for 2026-28, which is exactly why an undersupply call can run to 2Q28. Capital returns sized: [[SAMSUNG]] W30tn+ special dividend / W40tn+ buyback 1H27 / W30tn year-end; SK Hynix 50% of FCF weighted to buybacks (W40tn+ vs W20tn+).**
