  **🔴🔴 THE HEADLINE: NVIDIA INSTITUTIONALISES THE FUNDING GAP.** [[NVDA]] formally announced financing platforms with **Apollo, BlackRock, Blackstone, Brookfield, Goldman Sachs and KKR** to *"mobilize over **$500 billion of third-party capital**"* on the premise that **"NVIDIA compute is an investable asset"** (NVIDIA Newsroom, 17:12 ET; FT broke it mid-day). **The structural point is that, unlike the ~$70bn of ecosystem stakes, the ~$250bn OpenAI backstop and the Lancium stake logged this morning, this capital does NOT sit on NVDA's balance sheet** — *"a major tailwind for demand without filling up Nvidia's balance sheet."* 🔴 **The competitive read is the part that changes share models: "AI chip firms are NO LONGER SIMPLY COMPETING ON TECHNICAL SPECIFICATIONS… increasingly, CAPITAL IS A CRITICAL PIECE OF THE VALUE PROPOSITION as firms contemplate whether to pick Nvidia, AMD, TPU, or someone else," with [[GOOG]]/[[AVGO]] "running in second place"** (Vital Knowledge / Crisafulli). ⚠️ **NOT ADOPTED AS COMMITTED CAPITAL: per-partner amounts, tenor, pricing ("attractive rates" is unquantified) and any NVDA first-loss are all undisclosed — and The Information's July audit of the SK announcement ("NVDA will not say which company is putting up how much") is the governing precedent. Carried as MOBILIZATION CAPACITY.**

  **🔴 THE SECOND-BIGGEST ITEM IS AN INTEL PRIMARY-ADJACENT SOURCE, AND IT IS THE RICHEST INTC MATERIAL SINCE THE Q2 PRINT.** The **$15bn follow-on roadshow call with CFO David Zinsner** re-frames the raise from opportunistic to **mechanical**: *"both of Lip-Bu Tan's preconditions — **process proof and demand conviction** — have been met"*; Intel *"**won't place purchase orders without identified funding**"* and will **issue POs across multiple equipment vendors within the next few weeks**, against tools/shell/**billions in substrate prepayments** landing within **~18 months** while the ITC arrives later. Equity-only **to protect the IG rating, "which matters to foundry customers, not just rating agencies."** 🔴 **14A drew the most confident language of the session — "a STRAIGHTFORWARD SHRINK with the hardest engineering (GAA, backside power) already done," defect-density trajectory "THE BEST INTEL HAS SEEN SINCE 22nm," with the 0.9 PDK to be CONFIRMED ON THE LATE-OCTOBER EARNINGS CALL** (risk production 2027, volume 2028); 18A *"meeting or beating its ~7% monthly defect-density improvement target."* **Demand: 10+ CPU LTAs (3-5yr, some with share and pricing components); "CPU-to-GPU ratio near parity"; Pitzer sized edge/physical AI (~10% of client CPU revenue) as potentially rivaling the ENTIRE traditional PC market** — ⚠️ **the exact opposite of BofA's "pure BS" call on the same ratio argument, now logged side by side on the wiki as an unresolved debate.** **Foundry conversations have moved "from monthly yield reviews to WEEKLY COMMERCIAL NEGOTIATIONS over terms and capacity corridors"; customers "could also contribute capital in some form"; [[SPCX]]'s 14A interest cast as an endorsement of node maturity.** ⚠️ **Break-even still exit-2027 but "could slip into 2028," which management PRE-FRAMED as a demand-driven positive — logged as framing, not fact.** Model inputs: **$5 front-end capex per $1 back-end; ASICs $2B → $4B run-rate; Mobileye ~78% / Altera ~48% as monetization levers; "old Intel was a RULE OF 30 company, the new Intel targets a RULE OF 45 on a ~15x LARGER TAM."**

  **🔴 WFE GETS ITS BIGGEST REVISION OF THE CYCLE — AND TWO INDEPENDENT METHODS CONVERGE.** **Bernstein (David Dai) lifts 2026 WFE to $148bn (+26.3%) from $141bn, 2027 to $204bn (+32.6%) from $175bn, and 2028 to $259bn (+27%) from $198bn — a two-year growth of +75%**, *"across all applications, but slightly more from **non-China memory** and **China foundry/logic**"*; by application 2027/28 **DRAM +53%/+37%, NAND +58%/+42%, Logic-Foundry +20%/+19%**. **UBS (Arcuri) gets to the same place bottom-up off [[LRCX]]'s print: "WFE will be run-rating ~$170-175B in CQ4… likely to EXCEED (and maybe materially) $200B next year, with $250B becoming an increasingly PEDESTRIAN number for C2028."** ⚠️ **Two unrelated builds landing within a few billion at both 2027 and 2028 is the strongest evidence this wiki has had that ~$200bn 2027 is not one house's enthusiasm.** ⚠️ **But two contradictions are now explicit and left unresolved on purpose: (a) Bernstein has NAND WFE growing FASTER than DRAM in both years — the identical observation Jefferies used on 08-07 to build the BEARISH LTA case ("someone's adding capacity in NAND, likely the Koreans, earlier than anticipated"); (b) a +27% WFE year in 2028 sits against the standing "2028: DRAM −8% / NAND −14%" device down-leg on the hbm-memory page.** [[AMAT]] preview bogey: **full-year semi-systems guide from "up more than 30%" to "up 35-40% y/y (or more)" (UBSe +38%), implying >250bps of WFE share gain this year**, with **GM upside to 54-55%** the metric to watch; UBS C26/27/28 EPS **$15.15 / $23.14 / $30.15**, PT $705.

  **⚠️ THE RUBIN ULTRA DE-SPEC RE-OPENS — AND THIS PARTIALLY WALKS BACK THE "SETTLED" FRAMING ADOPTED ON 08-07.** MS Asia (Charlie Chan): *"Rubin Ultra will feature **SEVERAL SKUs** with high-end adopting **HBM4e 8-Hi** and lower-end using **HBM4 12-Hi or 8-Hi**"* on capacity/cost/workload grounds — **with the final decision NOT YET MADE ("before end-3Q26")**. 🔴 **Net-new and the most useful line anyone has written on the de-spec: "HBM de-specification will have a MORE MEANINGFUL IMPACT ON DECODING THAN PRE-FILL WORKLOADS, PARTICULARLY FOR LONG-CONTEXT-WINDOW LLMs"** — i.e. it bites exactly the workload class the tokenmaxxing page has growing fastest. ⚠️ **And it is flagged to spread: "we do not rule out MAJOR ASIC PROJECTS adopting different HBM configurations for similar reasons."** **On racks: PCB and thermal challenges mean first-gen Rubin Ultra racks "likely continue to use the OBERON solution for NVL72," with CPO/NPO reserved for NVL576-scale** — which resolves the wiki's standing "CPO is in mass production" vs "the near-term architecture is NPO" pair into a **packaging-tier split, and dates the CPO revenue leg to NVL576.**

  **🔴 [[AAPL]] — JEFFERIES GOES TO SELL, AND THE MECHANISM IS A CANCELLED PRODUCT.** **THESIS DRIFT applied: ~~HOLD, TP $285.50~~ → SELL, PT ~$263; FY28E/FY29E EPS −2.1% / −3.4%** (Edison Lee). **The claim: the ALL-GLASS iPHONE slated for Sep-2027 has been CANCELLED on low yield — "the major complexity is in producing the GLASS MIDFRAME" — a phone estimated at a US$2,060 BLENDED RETAIL ASP, "a major setback to efforts to bring in higher-priced iPhones amid soaring memory costs."** ⚠️ **This is the missing leg of the cost story: the wiki already carried memory as a "100-year flood", +10% foundry pricing, and CXMT refusing Apple a price cut — what was missing was the OFFSET, and Edison's call is that the offset has been withdrawn, leaving the inflation unhedged.** Also: **trade-in values raised +5% US / ~2% Europe** (pull-forward into iPhone 17, pressure on 18). ⚠️ **SINGLE-SOURCE supply-chain check, uncorroborated — the page's top verification item, not a fact.** ⚠️ **[[GLW]] read-through bounded by the analyst himself: "the glass midframe issue seems to be more of an AAPL and assembly issue than an issue with GLW directly"** — a forgone content opportunity, not a lost socket. ✅ **Separately, the Kumamoto question CLOSED by JPM: TSMC did see ISP fab disruption for Apple (through Sony), but "backup capacity in its Taichung Fab should have been able to pick up the slack, resulting in NO MEANINGFUL IMPACT to iPhone 18 timelines."**

  **🔴 [[SPCX]] — THE FIRST BOTTOM-UP CURSOR MODEL, AND IT IS A REVENUE-PER-WATT ARGUMENT.** MS (Adam Jonas, OW $300 / bull $600, ref $139): backing out **Space + Connectivity ($127 MSe)** implies **the whole AI business at ~$12/share, "slightly more than 1x 2028 EV/Sales, well below even neocloud peers."** Cursor ARR **~$4bn (June) → ~$8bn YE26 → $17bn '27 → $33bn 2030**, contributing **~$2.5bn (2026) and $13bn (2027)** = ~10% / ~19% of MS's AI revenue — **while consuming only 7-10% of SpaceXAI nameplate compute, "nearly TWICE the revenue intensity per watt of the average SpaceXAI workload."** GM **negative → positive in 3Q26 → ~low-60s by 2030**. The **$60.0bn all-stock Cursor acquisition closes in 3Q26**; **Grok 4.5 was jointly trained on Cursor interaction data**; Cursor is used by **>64% of the Fortune 500**. ⚠️ **NOT ADOPTED: "this model has NOT been vetted by management," Cursor is private, and the $33bn assumes NONE of the broader platform.** Corroborating third-party adoption data (**Ramp AI Index: median monthly AI spend/employee ~$11 in June, +167% y/y, top 1% >$4,880; ~55% of US businesses now pay for AI; Jellyfish: median company still ships <30% of code with AI help**) is checkable and is filed on tokenmaxxing.

  **🔴 THE NON-AI SIDE OF THE CYCLE CRACKS, AND THE SHORTAGE MOVES OFF MEMORY.** **Barclays (Tom O'Malley) cuts PCs to CY26 −14% (from −10%) and CY27 −3%, modelling Q3 and Q4 both −11% q/q vs +5%/−3% seasonality** — *"July came in well below… following a large beat in June which cited **PULL-INS**… a couple more ODMs are now pointing to **DD% declines for the year**,"* with component tightness *"the one consistent message."* ⚠️ **First clean evidence that memory/component inflation has crossed from a MARGIN event into a VOLUME event in the largest non-AI end market.** Against it, the AI chain printed its best month on record: **CASCI composite 145.3, +4.4pts, the largest gain in six months**, with server assembly rebounding — and Culpan's framing is the mis-positioning call: *"**shortages of memory… have taken attention away from tightness elsewhere. Investors who obsessed over memory and ignored other components are now feeling the effects of their blindspot**"* (passives accelerating; **[[TSM]]'s +32% July USD growth "relatively MILD" vs other component makers**; midstream backlogged on raw materials and equipment — *"**money alone isn't enough**"*).

  **NET-NEW SINGLE-NAME SUBSTANCE.** **[[LITE]] prints 08-11 into three independent setups: UBS Neutral $960 is the only formal caution and its bear is a COMPS bear — "growth in the Sept and Dec quarter is likely to be 'PEAKISH'" — with the DecQ guide ($1,150-1,200M, ~120% y/y) the actual event; ⚠️ UBS also cites "INCREASING SUPPLY OF InP" as pressuring the multiple, directly against this wiki's substrate-squeeze thesis.** MS (Meta Marshall) previews both optical names cautiously, **prefers [[COHR]] on margins**, and warns *"this set of results will not solve the longer-term debates: CPO ramp execution and margin durability."* **Japan's optical season closed with Fujikura's SECOND above-consensus guide hike in under two months (FY OP ¥432bn vs cons ¥330bn) and Furukawa's 1Q OP 50% above cons**; ⚠️ **Sumitomo Electric's miss was a capacity-expansion outage, not demand.** **[[CSCO]] prints 08-12 with UBS Buy $132 and BofA Buy — both see the beat; UBS is 3% BELOW consensus on the Oct guide, and caps GM "around 66%" on component costs; BofA's FY26 AI bogeys: hyperscaler AI orders ~$9B (from $2B) and AI revenue ~$4B, with the question being how confidently CSCO talks to ~$6B in FY27.** **[[MSFT]] — The Information reports Maia production ramping sharply in 2027 targeting EXTERNAL customers "like [[ANTHROPIC]]"** ⚠️ (lede only, nothing else disclosed; in tension with Anthropic's TPU+Trainium commitments), with **[[MRVL]] +4% on it** ⚠️ **though neither source names Marvell as the partner.** **[[GOOG]] — GS (Sheridan) reiterates Buy $435 and reads the reorg as a deliberate shift "from a research-led AI organization into a SCALED AI PLATFORM COMPANY," the direct counter-frame to SemiAnalysis's 08-06 write-off — note both AGREE that GCP is the value node.** **[[NBIS]] prints 08-12 (BofA Buy $280) with a dated permitting risk: the Vineland NJ EXPANSION hearing RESUMES AUGUST 17th, with the revised design "primarily powered by [[BE]] fuel cells"** ⚠️ — first named instance of data-center opposition attaching to a listed operator's specific site with a calendar date; **and the 07-15 "infra partner"/asset-light model remains unsized by any house.** **[[CRWV]] prints 08-11 (BofA Buy $140) on an ACTIVE power bogey of 1.0GW → 1.7GW by year-end** ⚠️ (active ≠ contracted). **[[AMAT]] 08-13, [[TSM]] July revenue re-underwritten by JPM (OW NT$3,200; 3Q26 GM ~66.5%, −1.2pts q/q on N2 dilution; capex US$81bn/US$90bn FY27/28; CoWoS +85%/+65%; ⚠️ JPM takes the other side of the wiki's EMIB-T thesis — "we don't see Intel EMIB-T posing a material threat to TSMC CoWoS").** **[[ASML]] — BofA's baseline productivity number lands (230 wph today → >500 wph roadmap) and the risk is CONDITIONED not dismissed: "a VERTICALLY INTEGRATED challenger combining a superior source with differentiated optics, stages and process technology could be a LT risk, although unlikely within the next decade."** **Advanced packaging split the inspection names on the same day: Jefferies BUY Camtek $200 ($600M YTD orders vs $255M revenue; Sept guided $159M vs St $148.8M; AP +45% h/h) against MS EW $165 on relative growth (CAMT AP +39% vs ONTO 80%, LAM/KLA 70%, NVMI 50%) with the diagnosis being LOWER TSMC EXPOSURE — "which should set the company up for a better 2027 once CoWoS builds BROADEN to ASE, SPIL, AMKR."**

**Headline events:** (1) **MS · Adam Jonas reframes [[SPCX]]'s 10GW question from CHIPS to POWER** — chip supply "mostly secured" post the NVDA exclusivity, MS models **5 GW by YE27 vs Musk's 10 GW** because of off-grid power, and the bottleneck is named one layer below the turbine: **hot-section blades and vanes, three casters worldwide, all backlogged**, with a reconstructed SpaceX foundry in Bastrop TX that MS says is **2-3+ years from volume**. (2) **SemiAnalysis (via Jefferies) cuts 2026 [[AVGO]] TPU CoWoS to 215k wafers from 250k for a PACKAGING reason (CoWoS-S ramp), not a demand reason** — Ironwood to 2.7mn units from 3.2mn with output now **peaking in 2Q26 and declining in 2H26** — while **[[META]]'s 55k 2027 wafers emerge as the relinquishable block** that could flow back to [[GOOG]]. (3) **TPU v10 goes CUSTOMER-OWNED-TOOLING**: JPM has [[MEDIATEK]] as likely primary back-end partner under semi-CoT with Alchip/GUC on portions, and SemiAnalysis reports "industry chatter" that **[[AMD]] is in for CPU IP / SoIC / packaging** — with the hard supporting datapoint that **TPU v8i runs 1 Axion CPU per 2 TPUs vs 1 Intel Xeon per 4 on v7**. (4) **MS · Nowak publishes the first sized bridge from Jassy's "$1trn AWS" remark to a share price** (~8 incremental GW/yr, **$8 → $12-15 of REVENUE per incremental watt**, 30% LT EBIT margin, ~$500/share by YE27; base PT unchanged at $335). (5) **[[SMIC]] gets a third house on the Q2 print and the disagreement is entirely about DEPRECIATION** — JPM Neutral **PT HK$67 → HK$78** modelling depreciation +30%/+20% vs revenue +27%/+16% and GM stuck at 24-26%, vs Jefferies BUY HK$88 and MS OW HK$85.

## 2026-08-19 · /wiki-ingest (scheduled, 21h) · scope = all pages with new material · sources: 505 Outlook emails screened → 89 whitelist hits → 49 bodies pulled · 784 tweets screened · 0 new transcripts · **26 company pages + 8 theme pages touched**

**Headline:** three unrelated majors on one tape — **[[ADI]] printed its first $4bn quarter with an FQ4 guide 5-8% above the Street (intra-quarter window RESET)**; **[[GOOG]] signed [[MRVL]] as a third TPU silicon partner, paid in a 58.97mn-share warrant vesting to ~$120bn of cumulative revenue through Jan-2033**; and **[[SKHYNIX]] approved a W40trn (~$29bn) buyback-and-cancellation with the FCF payout ceiling lifted from "within 50%" to "over 50%"** — all into a **−5.6% SOX / −5.8% KOSPI** session whose stated trigger was the US long-bond sell-off plus an [[ANTHROPIC]] ARR figure (~$65bn end-July) that came in below extrapolation.

- **Company pages:** ADI · MRVL · GOOG · AVGO · SKHYNIX · SAMSUNG · CEREBRAS · NVDA · TSM · MU · AMD · MEDIATEK · INTC · CDNS · SNPS · PLTR · CRM · NBIS · SPCX · TSLA · PANW · CRWD · TER · ADVANTEST · ANTHROPIC · OPENAI.

The tool now encodes first and only then opens.** (2) 🔴 **A SECOND, subtler tooling bug: the first version of the theme-page patcher read to `str`, split on `\\n` and re-joined with ONE line ending, which silently rewrote every line whose terminator differed and produced phantom diffs of up to −13 lines on files that are legitimately MIXED CRLF/LF. Caught by auditing `git diff --numstat` for non-zero deletions, reverted, and the patcher rewritten to operate on BYTES, keeping each surviving line's own terminator and stamping only the dominant EOL on inserted lines. **Final audit: 510 insertions / 0 deletions across 40 files on the additive pass**, with the only deletions in the whole run being the 7 deliberate in-cell Sinal-row replacements (NVDA ×3, GOOG ×2, MRVL ×1, AAPL ×1) and the 12 expected value updates in `sources_through.json`.** (3) **`sources_through.json` is CRLF and was patched by targeted regex on values only — 11 dates advanced, 2 new keys (NVT, PWR), JSON re-validated after write.** (4) ⚠️ **Sinal-vs-gestão tables were re-evaluated on FOUR pages only (NVDA, MRVL, GOOG, AAPL) — the pages where today's flow bears directly on a logged management claim.
